### FEATURE: workflow-gate: docs step should recognize WORKTREE_NOTES.md ## History Notes / ## Changelog Notes as docs evidence (post-#436 residual gap) (2026-05-23, 912da07601d7f2621948dc7c02db7ff15b8f30cd, #484)
Background: Under `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on`, `/update-docs` stages history/changelog entries into `WORKTREE_NOTES.md` `## History Notes` / `## Changelog Notes` (gitignored) instead of writing `docs/history.md` / `CHANGELOG.md` directly. The deferred write happens during `/worktree-end` Step 6i via `bin/compose-doc-append-entry` (introduced by #436 / PR #474). However, `workflow-gate.js` (and `workflow-mark.js` around line 527) still requires staged `docs/*` or `*.md` evidence to pass the docs gate, and explicitly rejects `MARK_STEP_docs_complete`. Result: installer-only or code-only fixes that legitimately have no `docs/` or root-level `*.md` change cannot satisfy the docs gate under worktree mode, even though `/update-docs` did its job by staging into `WORKTREE_NOTES.md`. Repro: PR #483 (fix #471, merged 2026-05-23) was an installer-only fix; `/update-docs` staged bullets into `WORKTREE_NOTES.md`; commit blocked at docs gate. Workaround applied: artificial 1-line `README.md` update. This is the natural next residual gap after #436 — the new staging surface is invisible to the gate.
Changes: Under `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on`, teach `workflow-gate.js` to accept a non-empty `## History Notes` or `## Changelog Notes` section in `<worktree>/WORKTREE_NOTES.md` as valid docs evidence. Read-only check (no staging required, since the file is gitignored). Acceptance: - Installer/code-only fixes that update `WORKTREE_NOTES.md` notes sections pass the docs gate without an artificial doc edit. - Existing `docs/*` / `*.md`-staged evidence still passes. - Empty notes sections (default placeholder bullet) do not satisfy the gate. - `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=off` path is unchanged (still uses `doc-append` directly).


### FEATURE: fix: restore code plan-file open with --folder-uri workspace targeting (counter-patch #454, closes #291) (2026-05-23, 866fec01a7be9fd3f0a9a283efee70d8db95ff09, #486)
Background: PR #454 (merged 2026-05-22, closes #445) removed `code -r` auto-open from `hooks/show-plan-link.js` unconditionally to satisfy #445's intent of suppressing VS Code file open when `CONFIRM_*=off`. The removal was over-broad — even when `CONFIRM_*=on` (default), intent/outline/detail.md no longer appear in any VS Code window; only the URL breadcrumb shows in the main conversation. The user has no convenient way to view the plan file before Proceed/Revise. In parallel, #291 (OPEN since 2026-05-14) describes that `code -r` without workspace targeting routes to the most recently focused VS Code window — not the originating workspace — when multiple VS Code windows run workflow sessions. Since #454 removed `code -r` entirely, #291 is temporarily moot, but any restoration of `code` invocation must address #291 at the same time or the bug returns.
Changes: Restore VS Code file open as a forward-patch (not a revert of #454), addressing #291 at the same time. ### Modified - `hooks/show-plan-link.js`: restore `code` spawn for VS Code file open, gated by `CONFIRM_*=on` (preserves #445's intent that off-mode does not open files), VS Code environment detection (`TERM_PROGRAM === "vscode"` or `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT === "claude-vscode"`), and `SHOW_PLAN_LINK_NO_AUTO_OPEN=1` opt-out. - Pass `--folder-uri` (or equivalent workspace-targeting argument) to address #291 — open in the VS Code window for the originating workspace, not the most recently focused window. - Apply to all 3 plan artifacts (intent.md / outline.md / detail.md — sibling class members handled by the same hook). - Windows CVE-2024-27980 workaround: `spawn("cmd.exe", ["/d","/s","/c","code",...])` on Windows. - `SHOW_PLAN_LINK_NO_SPAWN=1` + `SHOW_PLAN_LINK_MARKER_FILE` test bypass to be restored. - `skills/_shared/confirm-plan.md`, `.env.example`, `docs/architecture/claude-code/settings.md` updated. - Tests in `tests/feature-show-plan-link.sh`: code spawn + window-targeting cases. ### Explicitly NOT changed - `hooks/show-diff.js` diff suppression for `CONFIRM_*=off` (introduced in #454) — kept as-is to minimize the counter-patch. - `hooks/lib/plan-confirm-flag.js` (introduced in #454) — reused as-is for `CONFIRM_*` SSOT. - No `git revert` of #454 — forward-patch only, so downstream history (#462 etc.) is not disturbed. ## Closes - #291 — multi-VS-Code-window routing addressed by `--folder-uri` ## Related - #445 (closed by #454) — original `CONFIRM_*=off` suppression goal, preserved - #425 (open) — write-tests `CONFIRM_TESTS=OFF` (not in scope) <!-- auto-created by clarify-intent — session 20260523-133235 -->

Background: When two VS Code windows are open simultaneously and each runs a workflow session (for different repositories), the plan file (e.g. outline) meant for one window opens in the other. The `code` command routes to the most recently focused VS Code instance rather than the workspace that originated the request. ## Steps to Reproduce 1. Open VS Code window A (repo: `agents`) 2. Open VS Code window B (repo: another repo) 3. Start a workflow session in both windows simultaneously 4. Observe: window A may receive the outline file intended for window B ## Desired Behavior Each workflow session's plan files open in the VS Code window that corresponds to the originating workspace / repository. ## Notes - Root cause likely: `code` without `--folder-uri` or `--reuse-window` picks the most recently active window - Fix candidates: pass `--folder-uri "$PWD"` to target the correct workspace, or use `code -r` with the workspace path - Related: the environment detection issue (#290) addresses the non-VS Code case; this issue focuses on multi-window VS Code disambiguation --- *Auto-created by clarify-intent — session 20260515-021319*

### FEATURE: workflow: granular plan-skip sentinels (WORKFLOW_OUTLINE_NOT_NEEDED / WORKFLOW_DETAIL_NOT_NEEDED) for trivial fixes (2026-05-23, 5aecc52f1fc14b513ec9a4b9d11966262a62ffb2, #485)
Background: The current workflow has a single coarse-grained plan-skip sentinel: `WORKFLOW_PLAN_NOT_NEEDED`. For trivial fixes (1-file installer additions, typo fixes, copy-paste-style changes), running the full three-stage planning pipeline (intent → outline → detail) is disproportionate overhead — but skipping it entirely with `WORKFLOW_PLAN_NOT_NEEDED` is also wrong, because intent capture still adds value (it records `closes_issues`, scope, and acceptance criteria). In practice CC ends up running the full pipeline even for trivial fixes because there is no fine-grained skip handle.
Changes: Introduce sub-stage skip sentinels: - `WORKFLOW_OUTLINE_NOT_NEEDED: <reason>` — skip `/make-outline-plan` when only one obvious approach exists. - `WORKFLOW_DETAIL_NOT_NEEDED: <reason>` — skip `/make-detail-plan` when the outline already pins down file-level changes. Both must be **ask-gated** (require user approval, not auto-allow) so CC cannot self-emit them silently. Same gating posture as `WORKFLOW_PLAN_NOT_NEEDED`. Acceptance: - Each sub-stage sentinel passes its own gate independently; emitting one does not implicitly skip the others. - Reason field is mandatory (`: <reason>`); bare form rejected. - Telemetry / on-disk audit records which sub-stage was skipped and why. - `/clarify-intent` still runs in all cases (intent capture is non-negotiable). - Existing `WORKFLOW_PLAN_NOT_NEEDED` continues to skip both sub-stages together (backwards-compatible). ## Related - Use case: PR #483 (fix #471) was a 6-line-installer change where intent.md, outline.md, and detail.md were all generated for a one-line conclusion.

### FEATURE: /resume-session: findWorktreeEndMarker fails to detect existing pending-cwd-unlock marker from main worktree CWD (2026-05-23, d70e2085c3e1e668c03efc33f294e026a3f65a8e, #489)
Background: Verified in the field immediately after the release of PR #487 (new `/resume-session`). `/worktree-end` entered the deferred-cleanup path due to Windows EPERM, and with two valid markers written under `~/.workflow-plans/worktree-end/` — `pending-cwd-unlock-3ad563dbb2e32ad9--feature%2Fresume-session-468` and `...--feature%2F436-compose-doc-append` — a new session was started and `/resume-session` was run from the agents main worktree (`/c/git/agents`). Expected: `bin/resume-session-detect` returns `{type:"worktree-end-resume", marker_path:...}` and the skill dispatches to `/worktree-end --resume`. Actual: ```json {"type":"none","reason":"session_id_unresolved"} ``` `detect()` is structured to run `findWorktreeEndMarker()` first and immediately return `worktree-end-resume` on a hit. Falling through to `session_id_unresolved` means the marker scan returned null. repo-id `3ad563dbb2e32ad9` was computed from `getRepoId("C:/git/agents")` in the prior `/worktree-end` session and matches the filename. The CWD is `/c/git/agents` (the same main worktree, in MinGW notation). ## Suspected causes 1. **repo-id mismatch due to path-notation difference** (prime suspect) — the marker was written by hashing a backslash-form `C:\git\agents` base, while at detect time `process.cwd()` returns `/c/git/agents`, which is passed to the hash via `path.resolve` — the hash inputs may differ. An existing memory entry, `feedback_worktree_end_marker_repoid.md`, already documents this pitfall: "because `path.resolve` returns backslashes on Windows, repoId must be computed via `node -e "path.resolve(...)" | sha256sum`." 2. `readdirSync` succeeded but the case-insensitive comparison missed — unlikely since both sides go through `.toLowerCase()`. 3. `getWorkflowPlansDir()` is returning an unexpected directory — not impossible. Likely shares the same root cause as #293 (CLOSED, an identical mismatch pattern for the branch-delete marker: the writer hashes a raw relative path while the validator hashes a resolved absolute path).
Changes: - Align the `getRepoId(process.cwd())` call in `bin/resume-session-detect`'s `findWorktreeEndMarker()` with the same path normalization used on the marker-write side (`getRepoId(<absolute-main-root>)` in `skills/worktree-end` Step 6b). - Alternatively, change the marker storage schema: instead of a hashed repo-id, resolve via reverse-matching against the marker content (the absolute worktree path on Line 2). The latter would also structurally avoid the #293-class problem. - Add a regression test: add a test to `tests/feature-resume-session-468.sh` confirming that after writing a marker on Windows / Linux / macOS, it can be resolved via the path derived from `process.cwd()` (currently the `worktree-end-resume` path is smoke-tested only). - Also consider extracting the normalization rule suggested in `feedback_worktree_end_marker_repoid.md` into a shared function under `hooks/lib/` to make it the single source of truth. ## Repro 1. Windows + VS Code + Claude Code, `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on`. 2. Create a linked worktree via `/worktree-start`, make an edit, then run `/worktree-end`. 3. EPERM at Step 6c (easily reproduced if the VS Code extension host is holding the worktree CWD) → deferred marker write. 4. Without reloading VS Code, start another Claude Code session and run `/resume-session` from the agents main worktree. 5. Observe that the detect output is `type:"none", reason:"session_id_unresolved"` instead of `type:"worktree-end-resume"`.

### FEATURE: workflow-init: subagent artifact write failure & post-check verifies existence only (2026-05-23, 8b4e43bee4d0c37c4d4e2c73593e26f75ac631a7, #497)
Background: A recurring pattern has emerged where the Agent subagents launched in parallel at `workflow-init` Step 6 (`survey-code` / `survey-history`, etc.) skip the artifact write required by their SKILL.md and instead return the content as agent return text. Because the downstream `clarify-intent` / `make-outline-plan` skills are designed on the assumption that they will Read the artifact, the planner ends up making decisions on incomplete information. This is the same phenomenon reported by the earlier #419 — "the subagent gives up on writing to `~/.workflow-plans/` and returns text instead." This issue supersedes #419 and also scopes in the post-check detection gap (a request to close #419 as `status:migrated` will follow the creation of this issue). ### Observed facts (session-id: `75b0ee5b-9996-4f3e-bfae-6968d7066700`, 2026-05-23) | File | State | Content | |---|---|---| | `75b0ee5b-...-context.md` | present | normal (written by the orchestrator) | | `75b0ee5b-...-survey-code.md` | present | **only 1 line, `test`** (probe write) | | `75b0ee5b-...-survey-history.md` | absent | the Write call never fired | Both subagents' return text **fully contains the survey results that should have been written to the artifact**, so the problem is not content generation but a **misjudgment of the write mechanism**. ### Root cause (hypothesis) Reading both subagents' output: - They recognized that `enforce-worktree.js` blocks all Bash write-side commands - They **were not confident** in the judgment that the Write tool succeeds against `C:\Users\nire\.workflow-plans\` (outside the git repo), so they gave up on the file write and switched to a fallback mode of putting the body text into the agent return text instead - survey-code attempted a `test`-only Write as a probe (which succeeded) but finished without ever overwriting it with the real content - survey-history never even reached a Write call The fallback behavior for `agents/survey-code.md` / `agents/survey-history.md` when Bash writes are blocked is **not documented explicitly**. ### Secondary bug: post-check does not inspect content The post-check at `skills/workflow-init/SKILL.md` Step 6.5 is specified to check **only file existence**, so the 1-line `test` survey-code.md passes it. Even after the root cause is fixed, the post-check's content-verification gap remains as an independent issue.
Changes: Since the fix is a body-text change rather than a frontmatter change, plan it separately: 1. **Document explicitly in the subagent SKILL.md / agent.md files** (`agents/survey-code.md` / `agents/survey-history.md`, etc.):    - Add to the Rules section the fallback procedure for when Bash writes are blocked: "Write to `artifact_path` using the Write tool. Paths outside the git repo (e.g. `~/.workflow-plans/`) pass through enforce-worktree, so execute without hesitation." 2. **Content verification in the post-check** (`skills/workflow-init/SKILL.md` Step 6.5):    - Add a minimum file size (e.g. 200 bytes) **or** a grep-based check for the presence of a required section (`## Verified Claims`)    - On failure, emit `<<WORKFLOW_SURVEY_AGENT_FAILED: <name>>` (same fall-through as the existing spec) 3. **(Optional) orchestrator-side recovery** (under `bin/`):    - A CLI that restores content from the subagent's return text when the content is judged too thin ### Related memory - `feedback_cc_tool_env_var_handling.md` — known pitfall regarding subagent execution constraints - `feedback_verify_before_assert_or_act.md` — verification before asserting facts (in this case the agent skipped verification before concluding "write blocked") - `reference_claude_protected_paths.md` — `~/.workflow-plans/` is safe in all modes ### Supersedes - #419 (merged into this issue; a request to close it as `status:migrated` will follow the creation of this issue)


### FEATURE: show-plan-link: --folder-uri breaks file open in VS Code (regression from #490) (2026-05-23, 7b073d551ac254efaa08b3f7e8157a3a21c6a6d2, #506)
Background: When running the workflow, the `Plan file written: ...` breadcrumb is shown, but the intent/outline/detail.md tab does not open in VS Code. PR #490 (`fix(#486): restore code --folder-uri plan-file open; fix #291 multi-window routing`) introduced `--folder-uri` as a countermeasure for multi-window routing, but this broke the file-open behavior itself. In the intended case (VS Code environment + `CONFIRM_*=on`), the only effect that remains is the breadcrumb — everything else is broken. ### Reproduction (Windows 11, VS Code 1.121.0, `code .` from `C:\git\agents`) | # | Command | Result | |---|---|---| | 1 | `code --folder-uri file:///c:/git/agents -r <file>` | does not open | | 2 | `code -r <file>` | opens | | 3 | `code --folder-uri X --file-uri Y -r` | does not open | | 4 | `code --file-uri Y -r` | opens | When `--folder-uri` is supplied, the VS Code CLI processes only the folder open and silently drops the file open (neither the positional file nor `--file-uri`). Argument order and drive-letter case are irrelevant. ### Affected hook `hooks/show-plan-link.js:67` — `buildCodeArgs()` always inserts `--folder-uri` first. ### Test gap `tests/fix-restore-code-folder-uri.sh` verifies only the spawn argument string via `SHOW_PLAN_LINK_NO_SPAWN=1`, and does not verify whether VS Code actually opens the file. The E2E confirmation that "the tab actually opens" is missing.
Changes: Revisit the `--folder-uri` strategy in `hooks/show-plan-link.js` and change it to a form where file open works. ### Fix direction candidates (to be decided at the plan stage) - (A) Revert `--folder-uri` and go back to `code -r <file>` alone. Reconsider the `#291` multi-window problem via a different approach (reopen #291). - (B) Two-stage spawn: first `code --folder-uri X` (focus), then `code -r <file>` (open). - (C) Settle on opening in the last active window using `-r` alone, and split multi-window handling into a separate issue. ### Related work - #486 — closed by PR #490, but effectively unresolved by this issue - #291 — multi-window routing, closed at the same time by PR #490, but a reopen candidate if (A)/(C) is adopted - #492 — `workspaceFolderUriFrom` URI encoding (same `--folder-uri` path. If `--folder-uri` is reverted here, this may become wontfix) Related to #492 Related to #492


### FEATURE: show-plan-link: workspaceFolderUriFrom lacks URI encoding (2026-05-23, 7b073d551ac254efaa08b3f7e8157a3a21c6a6d2, #492)
Background: Surfaced by codex review on PR #490 (follow-up to #486 / #291). `hooks/show-plan-link.js` `workspaceFolderUriFrom()` builds `file://` URIs by raw string concatenation: ```js const fwd = cwd.replace(/\/g, "/"); return fwd.startsWith("/") ? "file://" + fwd : "file:///" + fwd; ``` Paths containing spaces, `#`, `%`, `?`, non-ASCII characters, or UNC/network forms (`\server\share`) produce invalid or misinterpreted `file://` URIs. This breaks the `--folder-uri` workspace matching introduced in PR #490, which was supposed to fix multi-window mis-routing (#291).
Changes: - Fix `workspaceFolderUriFrom()` to URI-encode the path portion (per-segment `encodeURIComponent` preferred — `encodeURI` leaves `?`/`#` unescaped, both valid in filenames but URI-reserved). - Handle UNC paths (`\server\share` → conventional `file://server/share/...` form, not the current 4-slash output). - Add test coverage in `tests/feature-show-plan-link.sh` for: spaces, `#`, `%`, non-ASCII, UNC. Current PR #490 tests cover only ASCII forward-slash paths.


### INCIDENT: #3: gh issue create is blocked from the main worktree (inconsistent with memory) (2026-05-23, a44c8c9423200ac242a85dd30157d98045d6b676, #369)
Cause: `hooks/lib/bash-write-patterns.js` overrides classification to read for the Group A `gh` administrative commands (`pr`/`issue`/`repo` create/edit/close/comment) only when solely quoting-only patterns match (here-doc/here-string/pwsh-here). However, when the body contains a POSIX-redirect-equivalent string (e.g. `>` `>>`) or matches some other non-quoting pattern, the override condition `matchedNames.every(QUOTING_ONLY)` becomes false, and `classify` returns write. `enforce-worktree.js` line 1330 then blocks the write from the main worktree. The memory entry `project_enforce_worktree_gh_scope.md` stated that "gh issue create / comment / edit can be run from the main worktree," but in practice it is blocked depending on body content.
Fix: One of the following directions needs to be decided (not implemented in this issue — to be addressed in a separate session): (1) Relax the Group A command override condition to ignore body-content patterns (via `stripGhArgs`, which strips tokens recognized as flag arguments such as `--title`/`--body`) (2) Correct the documentation/memory to match reality: "blocked depending on body content" (3) Provide a wrapper script for the main worktree that calls the gh API directly instead of Bash `gh` invocation. Reproduction condition: in this session, when attempting to create issue #368, the `gh issue create` whose body contained Markdown notation/code examples was blocked by ENFORCE_WORKTREE (worked around by emitting `<<WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKTREE_OFF: ...>>`). Related: #368 (autonomous /loop incident — this issue is a secondary problem discovered while creating #368). --- Fix task: #371 (sub-issue) — a concrete fix proposal (stripping the heredoc body for Group A commands so it is excluded from the write-pattern check) and refinement of the root cause.

### FEATURE: bug: write-code skill ignores CONFIRM_CODE=off and pauses after step 6 (2026-05-23, 03cde1f03d7e289aaa58da61e0ecce3813da976f, #472)
Background: After completing the `/write-code` skill with `CONFIRM_CODE=off`, Claude stops and emits a completion summary instead of automatically proceeding to Step 6 (run-tests + security reviews). The user had to ask "Did I tell you to stop here?" to surface the behavioral error. ### Observed in session session-id: 20260523-015103 (issue #436 implementation) After 10 source files were edited successfully by the write-code subagent, Claude posted a completion summary listing the edited files and paused — instead of auto-proceeding to Step 6. This is the same pause-when-CONFIRM-off pattern as #425 (write-tests skill, Step 7), but in /write-code Step 6. #425's 'Related skill' section explicitly predicted write-code as one of the likely recurrences. ### Why this is wrong `skills/write-code/SKILL.md` does not instruct Claude to wait or to announce completion as a stop point: - Step 2 (CONFIRM_CODE gate): explicit OFF path proceeds without confirmation. - Step 6: 'Present the final edited file list + skipped-check notes + scope-expansion notes to the user.' - Completion section: 'None.' CLAUDE.md workflow Step 5 → Step 6 implies auto-progression. The user has a standing preference (recorded in memory) that CONFIRM_*=off must auto-proceed at every review point without pausing. ### Root cause hypothesis Step 6's 'Present the final edited file list to the user' is ambiguous — same wording-pattern problem as #425's analysis of write-tests Step 7. 'Present' is being read as 'pause for review' rather than 'emit summary and proceed'.
Changes: Tighten Step 6 wording, parallel to the #425 proposal for write-tests: - 'When CONFIRM_CODE=on, present the edited files for review and wait for approval. When CONFIRM_CODE=off, emit a one-line summary and auto-proceed to Step 6 (run-tests + security reviews) without waiting.' Alternatively, replace 'Present' with 'Emit summary and proceed' to eliminate the pause ambiguity entirely. A shared helper (`skills/_shared/pause-if-confirm-on.md`, mentioned as option 2 in #425) would address both this issue and #425 together. ## Related - #425 — same root cause in write-tests skill (Step 7); this issue is the empirical confirmation of #425's 'Related skill' prediction for write-code Related to #425 Related to #425

### FEATURE: worktree-end Step 7: structural countermeasure for the Final Report not being displayed (2026-05-24, 5c1016c6ca0e416fd07b50f33c47b3da47b048cf, #504)
Background: #405 introduced `bin/worktree-final-report.js` and templated worktree-end Step 7, but in actual operation the Final Report frequently fails to render as the template specifies. ### Confirmed causes 1. **Environment-variable hand-off failure (most frequent)**    - Launching the Step 7 renderer assumes that 10 env vars (`PR_NUMBER` / `PR_TITLE` / `PR_URL` / `PR_STATE` / `BRANCH` / `WORKTREE_PATH` / `CREATED_DATE` / `BACKUP_MANIFEST_PATH` / `BRANCH_DELETED` / `CLAUDE_CODE_RESTART_REQUIRED`) are passed via a single leading command    - The Windows Bash tool can reset CWD and env between commands (known; `cc_tool_env_var_handling`)    - If even one is missing anywhere along the long Step 5 → 5.5 → 6 → 7 path, the renderer output ends up full of `(none)`    - As a result, the model frequently falls back to hand-written Markdown 2. **No CLI enforcement (silent fallback)**    - `/run-tests` has a sentinel like `<<WORKFLOW_MARK_STEP_run_tests_complete>>`, but Step 7 has none    - Nobody detects it when the renderer crashes due to missing env / MODULE_NOT_FOUND, etc. 3. **Paths that never reach Step 7**    - Step 6c.1 deferred-fork (Windows CWD lock) → Stop, leaving Step 7 unexecuted    - There is no guarantee in SKILL.md that the `--resume` path re-runs Step 7 4. **main not pulled**    - Case where the main worktree right after a PR merge has not been pulled, so `bin/worktree-final-report.js` is not present → MODULE_NOT_FOUND
Changes: Improvement candidates (in priority order): | Option | Effect | Cost | |---|---|---| | A. Have the renderer emit `<<WORKFLOW_MARK_STEP_final_report_complete>>` at the end of stdout, and have the skill treat a missing sentinel as failure | Detects silent fallback | Low | | B. Write the env to `<plans>/<sid>-final-report-env.json` at the end of Step 5/5.5, and have Step 7 only read from it | Structurally eliminates env scattering | Medium | | C. Add a branch that always runs Step 7 even on `--resume` / deferred-fork recovery | Closes the gap | Medium | | D. At the start of Step 7, detect a missing renderer and attempt a single `git pull --ff-only` | Rescues the main-not-pulled case | Low | ### Proposal Combining (A) + (B) is the main structural approach. (C) and (D) can be split into separate issues. ### Related - #405 (the Final Report templating work itself) - #494 (adjacent problem where Step 6i/7 does not replay on the `--resume` path) Related to #494


### FEATURE: workflow-mark: session-scoped ENFORCE_WORKTREE_OFF sentinel produces no marker file (2026-05-24, 9d69f6bb1259f0e56d90458c8c0a1e4e84518230, #461)
Background: The `<<WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKTREE_OFF: <reason>>>` sentinel is sanctioned in worktree.md as a session-scoped enforcement bypass, but in the actual environment the marker file (`~/.workflow-plans/<session-id>.worktree-off`) is not created, and subsequent writes continue to be blocked. ### Reproduction From the main worktree: ``` echo '<<WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKTREE_OFF: temporary off for X>>>' ls ~/.workflow-plans/*.worktree-off   # expected: marker visible / actual: No such file # subsequent write commands → still blocked by ENFORCE_WORKTREE ``` ### Environment - session-id: 8f27a0e9-30af-4ae5-a294-ae8ba8797888 (this session) - VS Code extension Claude Code on Windows - agents repo: at the point PR #452 was merged into main - `echo $CLAUDE_ENV_FILE` → empty (Bash tool context) ### Hypothesis (Root cause) Around `hooks/workflow-mark.js` L640: ``` if (enforceOffMatch) {     if (!sessionId) {       messages.push("workflow-mark: could not resolve session_id — ENFORCE_WORKTREE override NOT applied.");       continue;     } ``` The sources for resolving `sessionId` are: 1. `input.session_id` — the hook's stdin JSON 2. `resolveSessionId()` — reads `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID=` from `CLAUDE_ENV_FILE` (`hooks/lib/workflow-state.js`) In this session, `$CLAUDE_ENV_FILE` is empty in the Bash tool context. If it is likewise empty in the hook context, `resolveSessionId()` returns null. The hook stdin's `input.session_id` is also not being passed (or is passed but skipped due to some other condition). As a result, `continue` fires at L640 and no marker file is written. Since the stderr `messages` are not surfaced into the Bash tool result seen by the user/Claude, the failure is silent. ### Impact - The "session-scoped escape hatch" sanctioned in worktree.md does not work - When the genuinely sanctioned path (main-worktree writes for issue filing or emergency recovery) is blocked by this hook issue, the only remaining options are editing `.env` or creating a separate worktree - Recent example: while filing #460, the sanctioned `/issue-create` path was blocked by a `>` character false-positive (the very symptom of #460) → an attempt was made to work around it via the sentinel, but the sentinel is also broken due to this issue → the user was asked to file it via the web UI instead (a two-stage degradation of the sanctioned path) - The silent-failure aspect is also a problem: no visible error is returned to the user/Claude when marker creation fails
Changes: - Root-cause isolation:   - Verify the `sessionId` resolution logic inside the sentinel handler in `hooks/workflow-mark.js` via debug logging (session_id propagation in the hook stdin JSON, the `CLAUDE_ENV_FILE` resolution path)   - Consider adding a fallback to `resolveSessionId()` (e.g. deriving the session id from `transcript_path`) - Visibility on failure:   - In addition to a final stderr flush of `messages`, surface a threshold at which the hook branches to a hard-fail stop (e.g. when only the marker write fails) via the `additionalContext` form   - Could also introduce a sanctioned API that lets Claude poll for marker existence immediately after emitting the sentinel (document in worktree.md) - Scope of impact: `hooks/workflow-mark.js`, `hooks/lib/workflow-state.js` (strengthen resolveSessionId), the `tests/feature-workflow-mark.sh` family, `rules/worktree.md` (document the failure procedure) Related to: - #369 (observed during follow-up issue creation for #369 obs (2)) - #460 (the very bug that required this sentinel to be functional) - Anthropic bug #27987 (`$CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` propagation issue — the root motivation for the marker-based bypass)


### FEATURE: worktree-end: retire pending-branch-delete- marker — replace with direct git worktree list check (2026-05-24, 79c96d54fe1c6dfd4c948954d266388790ab6785, #503)
Background: `pending-branch-delete-` markers (written by `worktree-end` Step 6b, read by `enforce-worktree.js` `isAllowedBranchDeleteViaMarker`) gate `git branch -D <branch>` from the main worktree. PR #499 introduced `/sweep-worktrees` to reclaim zombie worktrees and their branches via the sweeper, making the marker's purpose partially redundant. Codex review (PR #499) flagged a HIGH-severity cross-repo deletion risk: `sweep-worktrees.sh`'s orphan-marker pass decodes only the branch suffix from the marker filename and ignores the embedded repo-id. A stale marker from repo A can authorize `git branch -D` in repo B if they share $HOME/.workflow-plans. The correct fix: check `git worktree list --porcelain` directly to confirm a branch is not checked out in any worktree. No marker needed. This also makes #226 (session-binding enhancement for the same marker gate) obsolete. The `pending-cwd-unlock-` marker family was retired in PR #499. `pending-branch-delete-` is the remaining marker family. ### Additional scope: orphan-directory sweep Discovered during live verification immediately after PR #499 merged: Windows's `git worktree remove` is non-atomic, and the following intermediate state can be left behind — registered=false (already removed from the registry) while the physical directory remains (empty or nearly empty). Sequence: 1. `git worktree remove` first deletes the `.git` text file inside the worktree 2. The directory itself fails to delete with EPERM (e.g. CWD lock held by the VS Code extension host) 3. `/worktree-end` Step 6c.1 issues a WARN and skips Step 6d onward 4. A subsequent implicit prune (e.g. via `git fetch --prune`) reclaims the now-invalidated admin area → removed from the registry Result: a zombie that does not appear in `git worktree list` but leaves only a physical directory behind under `WORKTREE_BASE_DIR`. Since the current `sweep-worktrees.sh` scans starting solely from `git worktree list --porcelain`, this zombie is completely invisible to it. Concrete example: PR #499's own worktree `C:/git/worktrees/sweep-worktrees-pr1/agents` was left in this state immediately after merging.
Changes: - Remove `worktree-end` Step 6b (marker write) and Step 6g (marker delete); remove MARKER_PREFIXES.BRANCH_DELETE / getMarkerPath branch-delete usage - Add `isAllowedBranchDeleteWhenNotCheckedOut(cmd, repoRoot)` predicate to `enforce-worktree.js`: runs `git worktree list --porcelain` and allows `git branch -D <branch>` only when no worktree lists that branch as HEAD - Remove `isAllowedBranchDeleteViaMarker`, `isAllowedMarkerDelete`, `isMarkerFilePath` predicates from `enforce-worktree.js` - Remove orphan-marker 2nd pass from `sweep-worktrees.sh` (targets `pending-branch-delete-` only; now handled inline by the new predicate) - Update `skills/worktree-end/SKILL.md` to remove Steps 6b/6g - Update tests: replace marker-gate tests with worktree-list-based tests ### Changes (additional scope: orphan-directory sweep) - Add an orphan-dir scan pass to `sweep-worktrees.sh`: enumerate directories matching the `<task-name>/<repo-name>/` pattern under `WORKTREE_BASE_DIR`, and treat those not present in `git worktree list --porcelain` as orphan candidates - A 4-AND safety gate for orphan candidates:   1. The path resolves (normalized) under `WORKTREE_BASE_DIR` (path-traversal defense)   2. No `.git` file/directory exists inside the directory (prevents misidentifying a live worktree)   3. mtime > the `--min-age-hours` threshold (prevents accidentally deleting a manually created directory made within a short time)   4. Contents are empty, or contain only `WORKTREE_NOTES.md` (no uncommitted work files) - Reuse the existing `hooks/cleanup-orphan-dir.js`, which already has the orphan-dir deletion logic, from the sweeper (avoid duplicate implementation) - Actual deletion only happens with `--apply`; dry-run prints `would remove orphan dir: <path>` - Add an `orphan_dirs_removed: N` counter to the summary - Cross-repo safety per the Codex review HIGH finding (PR #499): since orphan-dir has no repo-id information, only target directories under `WORKTREE_BASE_DIR` whose `<repo-name>` matches the current repo's name (so directories from other repos are never accidentally swept) This extension is orthogonal to the marker mechanism's removal. Even after the marker is retired, the "Windows case where only the physical directory remains after a worktree is torn down" will continue to occur, so the orphan-dir sweep remains as an independent safety net. Related to #226 (supersedes its session-binding enhancement — marker removal makes the gate itself unnecessary)


### FEATURE: subagent: detail-planner / survey-code still attempt Bash heredoc writes to ~/.workflow-plans/ despite #507 (2026-05-24, bb653ece88bbca846c915f92769e177b297d85be, #516)
Background: Observed during the PR #510 session (fixes for #369/#460/#359) in progress. When the detail-planner and survey-code subagents write plan artifacts / survey artifacts under ~/.workflow-plans/, they used Bash heredoc / redirect (cat-heredoc-then-redirect-to-file / printf-quoted-then-redirect-to-file) instead of the Write tool, and were blocked by enforce-worktree.js (which blocks Bash writes from the main worktree). PR #507 (which closed #497) was supposed to have added an explicit instruction to subagent prompts to use the Write tool, but in this session the same phenomenon recurred even after that fix. ## Impact - The subagent's artifact write does not complete → the orchestrator has to do it instead, causing friction - In failure cases, the artifact is absent / left with stub content when passed to the downstream skill (same pattern as #497's root cause) - The enforce-worktree.js block itself is correct behavior (Bash redirect is a main-worktree write; the same applies to ~/.workflow-plans/ via Bash). The issue lies in insufficient behavioral instructions on the subagent side ## Suspected causes 1. PR #507's explicit instruction never reached the detail-planner.md / survey-code.md agent files (it may have only been added to the workflow-init or clarify-intent SKILL.md files) 2. The subagent prompt's instructions are abstract, and Bash heredoc has become the implicit default 3. The subagent is making an incorrect "safe-side" judgment about whether writing to ~/.workflow-plans/ is allowed (trying the Bash route and falling back) A known pattern also touched on in the memory entries feedback_cc_tool_env_var_handling.md / feedback_verify_before_assert_or_act.md.
Changes: Investigation scope: 1. Check whether the Write-tool-required instruction added in PR #507 has been reflected in all subagent definition files, including agents/detail-planner.md, agents/survey-code.md, agents/survey-history.md, agents/outline-planner.md, etc. 2. Add an explicit Rule wherever it is missing (use the Write tool / Bash heredoc is blocked / escalate if the artifact cannot be written) 3. Regression test: an E2E test confirming that the subagent actually calls the Write tool ## Acceptance - All plan-pipeline subagents, including detail-planner / survey-code / survey-history, use the Write tool to write under ~/.workflow-plans/ - They do not attempt Bash heredoc / redirect (verified by test) - The orchestrator no longer needs to write the artifact on the subagent's behalf ## Related - #419 (CLOSED, status:migrated to #497) - #497 (CLOSED, fixed by #507) - PR #507 (added the subagent artifact-write instruction; this issue addresses the recurrence after that fix) - memory feedback_cc_tool_env_var_handling.md, feedback_verify_before_assert_or_act.md, reference_claude_protected_paths.md

### FEATURE: worktree-end Final Report: classify restart/install necessity and always display the standard format (2026-05-24, 880cb0d3d6a9c686a36352131f1aeaaf44211551, #518)
Background: In the current worktree-end Step 7 Final Report, only a single flag, `CLAUDE_CODE_RESTART_REQUIRED`, is shown in the `### Claude Code Restart Required` section (introduced in #405 / #504). In actual operation there are two problems: ### Problem 1 — The section is dropped / falls back to free-form In #504, a structural countermeasure was put in place via the sentinel (`<<WORKFLOW_MARK_STEP_final_report_complete>>`) and the env-file, but even so: - Cases still frequently occur where the restart flag is internally set, but the restart section does not appear in the final output to the user - The user only gets "Yes, it is required" after explicitly asking "Is a restart required?" We want to structurally enforce that **the standard block is always emitted in the final response message**. ### Problem 2 — A single "restart" axis is insufficient Currently there is a single axis, "Claude Code restart," but in reality there are multiple "operations requiring reapplication" that differ in granularity and target, mixed together. Each of these should have its **own independent required / not-required flag** — they do not form a containment relationship or a severity ordering with each other. As examples, here are ones that come to mind right now (neither exhaustive nor ordered — the premise is that they will be **organized and adjusted** at design time): - **VS Code reload / restart** — when VS Code extensions, keybindings.json, or VS Code-side settings change - **Claude Code restart** — CLAUDE.md / rules/ / settings.json's `model`/`outputStyle`, etc. (the current `CLAUDE_CODE_RESTART_REQUIRED`) - **OS reboot** — registry changes, system PATH changes, etc. - **Re-running the target repo's installer** — the agents repo's `./install.ps1` / `./install.sh` (involving settings.json reconfiguration, symlink recreation, etc.) These 4 are not "any one of them is needed" but each **independent** — for example, combinations can occur such as "VS Code reload is needed but Claude Code restart is not" or "only re-running the installer is needed." Nor is there an intent to limit it to these 4: - There may be other applicable operation categories (e.g. docker compose's `up -d --build`, re-authenticating an external service, etc.) - Conversely, the categories might end up fewer after organizing (e.g. consolidating VS Code reload and Claude Code restart into a single "session-layer restart")
Changes: Direction: - Add a section to the Final Report that **lists "additional actions required after completion" with an independent required / not-required flag per category** - The section is **always shown, never omitted, even when every category is "no"** (making explicit that "nothing needs to be done") - Determine each category in `skills/worktree-end/lib/detect-restart.sh` (or a set of split libs), and when `yes`, also state "why" (the detected file/setting) - When even one category is `yes`, make it explicit in SKILL.md that the model must always restate a **highlighted standard summary block** immediately after the Final Report The concrete category breakdown and flag naming will be **finalized at the design phase**. The above 4 examples are a starting point, but may be reorganized at design time from the following angles: - Category consolidation (merging restarts that are essentially the same layer into one) - Category addition (operations currently missing) - Category removal (drop ones that are difficult to detect or give meaning to) The ultimate goal is a state where reading the standard block alone fully tells the user "what to do after merging." ## Constraints - Keep the existing sentinel `<<WORKFLOW_MARK_STEP_final_report_complete>>` and the env-file path - `detect-restart.sh` remains fail-safe (returns "no" when `gh` fails) - If the output format changes, `tests/fix-worktree-end-git-pull.sh` and the renderer tests need to follow suit - The backward-compatibility policy for the existing `CLAUDE_CODE_RESTART_REQUIRED` env var is to be decided at design time (deprecate / map / remove — one of the three) ## Accepted Tradeoffs - For categories that are difficult to auto-detect (e.g. OS reboot), allow the option of handling them via manual env only, treating auto-detection as future work - "Always show all categories" adds a few lines of noise even in the all-no case, but the value of explicitly stating "nothing needs to be done" is prioritized ## Related - #405 (Final Report template introduced) - #504 (structural countermeasure for Final Report not displaying — env-file + sentinel)


### FEATURE: cleanup(settings.json): audit and remove dead path-based allow/deny rules (2026-05-25, af0aff113272a1f828f87faade90409fa3118855, #254)
Background: Empirical results (Phase 1 diagnostic, 2026-05-13) and the 2026-05-04 history entry show that `settings.json` path-based allow/deny rules for `Read|Write|Edit|Grep|Glob` **do not reliably work in the VS Code Claude Code extension**. Terminal CLI (Mac/Linux) behaviour is unverified, but the hook-based mechanism (`block-dotenv.js`) works portably on all clients. The `settings.json` file therefore contains many path-based rules that may be either: - (a) Dead everywhere (already covered by a hook), or - (b) Effective on some clients but not VS Code, or - (c) Effective everywhere (Bash-pattern rules) ## Scope ### Audit table Enumerate every path-bearing `Read|Write|Edit|Grep|Glob(<glob>)` rule in `settings.json` (allow + deny) and classify each: - Hook coverage status (which hook, if any, enforces it) - VS Code extension status (effective / dead / unknown) - Terminal CLI status (effective / dead / unknown) - Action: remove / migrate to hook / keep with note Initial enumeration (non-exhaustive): | Rule | Hook coverage | VS Code | Term CLI | Action | |---|---|---|---|---| | `Read(**/.env.example)` allow (× 4 safe variants) | `block-dotenv.js` (silent for safe variants) | dead | likely effective | TBD: migrate safe-variant approval into hook? | | `Grep(**/.env.example)` allow (× 4 safe variants) | same | dead | likely effective | TBD | | `Read(**/.env)` deny (× 8 .env*) | `block-dotenv.js` blocks | redundant | redundant | remove | | `Grep(**/.env)` deny (× 8) | `block-dotenv.js` blocks | redundant | redundant | remove | | `Edit(**/.env)` deny (× 8) | `block-dotenv.js` blocks | redundant | redundant | remove | | `Write(**/.env)` deny (× 8) | `block-dotenv.js` blocks | redundant | redundant | remove | | `Read(**/.claude/plans/**)` allow | none | dead | likely effective | TBD | | `Write(**/tests/**)` allow + Edit | `block-tests-direct.js` (gatekeeper) | dead | likely effective | TBD | | `Write(~/.claude/plans/drafts/**)` allow (× 6 variants) | none → after #253 PR, covered by `approve-plan-writes.js` | dead | likely effective | remove after #253 PR ships | | `Read(~/.ssh/**)` deny | **none** | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** — add `block-credentials.js` hook? | | `Read(~/.gnupg/**)` deny | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** | | `Read(~/.aws/**)` deny | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** | | `Read(~/.azure/**)` deny | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** | | `Read(~/.config/gh/**)` deny | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** | | `Read(~/.git-credentials)` deny | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** | | `Read(~/.docker/config.json)` deny | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** | | `Read(~/.kube/**)` deny | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** | | `Read(~/.npmrc)` deny | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** | | `Read(~/.pypirc)` deny | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** | | `Read(~/.gem/credentials)` deny | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** | | `Edit(~/.bashrc)` deny (× 5 shell configs) | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** | | `Edit(~/.claude/projects/workflow/**)` deny + Write | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** — workflow state integrity | | `Edit(**/history.md)` deny + Write | none | likely dead | likely effective | **CRITICAL** — append-only enforcement | | `Write(**/.git/info/pending-branch-delete)` allow | none | dead (per Phase 1) | likely effective | TBD: check who actually writes this file | ### Verification protocol Before removing any deny rule, verify (manually or via E2E test): - On VS Code Windows: confirm the deny is currently NOT being enforced (Read/Edit/Write succeeds, possibly with a prompt). - On terminal CLI: confirm the deny IS being enforced (Read/Edit/Write fails outright). ### Migration plan For each `CRITICAL` row above, decide between: 1. **Add a `block-<category>.js` PreToolUse hook** (preferred for security-critical rules) 2. **Accept VS Code unprotected state, document the gap** (last resort) ## Why follow-up (not bundled with #253's PR) - Security review needed for any deny removal — out of immediate scope. - Some rules may need new hooks (`block-credentials.js`?) — separate design work. - Removing dead allow rules is mechanical but should be batched after hook coverage is in place. ## Non-goals - Do NOT remove `Bash(...)` allow/deny rules (different mechanism, known to work). - Do NOT remove `WebFetch(domain:...)` / `WebSearch` / tool-name allowlist (different mechanism).
Changes: Parent: #253


### FEATURE: refactor(hooks): extract path-match lib for shared path-normalization logic (2026-05-25, af0aff113272a1f828f87faade90409fa3118855, #252)
Background: Three PreToolUse hooks currently duplicate path normalization (backslash → forward slash, split into segments, basename extraction): - `hooks/block-dotenv.js` (~360 lines) — security-critical: blocks `.env*` access with Bash-injection-aware command parsing. Uses inline `filePath.replace(/\/g, "/").split("/")...` at least 5 times. - `hooks/block-tests-direct.js` (~50 lines) — **gatekeeper** (not pure approve): when `write_tests` workflow step is `pending`, blocks main-conversation writes to `tests/` to force `/write-tests` invocation; otherwise approves. Uses inline path normalization at line 47. - `hooks/approve-plan-writes.js` (new in #253's PR, ~20 lines) — UX: approves Write/Edit under `~/.claude/plans/`. Uses inline normalization initially. Codex single-pass architecture review (2026-05-13) concluded that the right pattern is **shared lib + separate hooks**, not consolidation: > Keep `block-dotenv.js` separate because its threat model and complexity are materially different from the UX-only approve hooks; mixing credential-protection logic with convenience approvals would make review harder and raise the blast radius of future changes. ## Scope 1. **Extract shared lib** — new `hooks/lib/path-match.js` containing:    - Path separator normalization (backslash → forward slash, win32-safe)    - Basename extraction    - Path segment iteration helper (`getPathSegments`)    - Home directory expansion (`~` → `os.homedir()`)    - Optional: case-folding helpers for Windows drive letters 2. **Refactor existing hooks to use the lib**:    - `hooks/block-dotenv.js` — replace inline basename/path normalization (5+ sites) with lib calls. Keep all Bash parsing (TEXT_FLAGS, PATH_FLAGS, shell wrapper recursion, command substitution) untouched. Security-critical — diff review required.    - `hooks/block-tests-direct.js` — replace `isUnderTestsDir` inline normalization with lib `getPathSegments`. Keep gatekeeper logic (workflow state check) untouched. ## Why follow-up (not bundled with #253's PR) - Keeps the immediate fix focused on the user-visible bug (plans/ dialogs on Windows). - `block-dotenv.js` is security-critical — refactor deserves its own review window with full test coverage verification. - The lib API can evolve based on usage in `approve-plan-writes.js` first before broader migration. ## Non-goals - Do NOT consolidate Bash parsing into the lib (too dotenv-specific; security-critical and complex). - Do NOT merge approve hooks into a single dispatcher (Codex verdict explicitly rejected). - Do NOT rename `block-tests-direct.js` — original assumption that it was a pure approve hook was wrong; the file's gatekeeper role justifies the existing `block-` prefix.

### FEATURE: wip-state.sh: CLAUDE_SESSION_ID does not propagate to the Bash subprocess, causing WIP signaling to stop entirely again (regression of #440) (2026-05-25, 3e23f64c8d7a088faa3391a833f49ebd1b08db4b, #519)
Background: ### Symptom The Projects v2 view (https://github.com/users/nirecom/projects/1/views/1) shows 0 issues with `Status: In Progress`. In reality there should be several in-progress issues (e.g. #503, #516), but they all remain at `Status: Todo`. ### Observation (2026-05-24, VS Code Claude Code session 058c78b2-906f-4866-8c1d-90f7d22314d8) Trace of `bash -x $AGENTS_CONFIG_DIR/bin/github-issues/wip-state.sh check 503`: ``` + load_env_file + . 'C:\git\agents/.env'           # WIP_STATE_*_ID loads normally + preflight_field_ids               # OK ++ resolve_session_id ++ '[' -n '' ']'                    # $CLAUDE_ENV_FILE is empty ++ '[' -n '' ']'                    # $CLAUDE_SESSION_ID is also empty ++ echo 'Error: CLAUDE_SESSION_ID not resolvable (neither $CLAUDE_ENV_FILE nor $CLAUDE_SESSION_ID is usable)' ++ return 2 + exit 2 ``` Comparison of env between the Bash tool's direct execution and a child process: ``` # Bash tool direct execution CLAUDE_ENV_FILE= CLAUDE_SESSION_ID=058c78b2-906f-4866-8c1d-90f7d22314d8 # child SUBSHELL process via bash -c CLAUDE_SESSION_ID= ``` → In the Bash tool's shell, `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` is **set as a variable but not exported**. It is not visible to a child-process invocation like `bash wip-state.sh`. ### Root cause The #440 (CLOSED 2026-05-22) fix added a `${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID:-}` fallback to `resolve_session_id`. The assumption at the time was that "the VS Code extension version of Claude Code exports the `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` environment variable directly to Bash subprocesses" (per the #440 issue body). However, in the current claude-vscode version, `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` is only set as a Bash-tool shell variable and is not exported, so it does not propagate to child processes. As a result, the #440 fallback is also unreachable, and `resolve_session_id` fails with rc=2. Since `clarify-intent/SKILL.md:96-98` defines "Exit 2 → warn and continue," the workflow is not interrupted, but **WIP set silently fails in every session**. ### Impact - The `workflow-init` Step 3(a) WIP check also fails with rc=2 for the same reason → treated as "advisory; warn-and-continue," so concurrent-session collision detection does not work - The `clarify-intent` Completion WIP set failure leaves the Projects v2 Status at `Todo` - `issue-close-finalize` Step K's `clear` also emits a "no changes to make" warning on fingerprint clear (since the fingerprint was never written in the first place) - **The #508 implementation itself (multi-N WIP loop, merged 2026-05-23) is correct, but has no effect because wip-state.sh does not work**
Changes: ### Fix direction (candidates) Add a session-id resolution path that does not rely on `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` propagation. Candidates: 1. Read the basename of the newest `.jsonl` under `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/` as the session-id (same path as #482's recovery procedure) 2. Via some other file/env-var that Claude Code always writes 3. Force `export CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` in the Bash tool wrapper (at the settings.json level? — needs investigation) Secondary: treat the case where `clear`'s `--text ""` returns "no changes to make" as a normal exit (also proposed in #440, not yet implemented). ### Test plan - Confirm that `bash -c 'echo $CLAUDE_SESSION_ID'` returns empty (current reproduction) - After the fix: confirm that `wip-state.sh set <N>` succeeds via the same Bash tool, and that `gh issue view <N>`'s Projects v2 Status transitions to `In Progress` - Regression: confirm continued operation via the legacy CLI Claude Code path (`CLAUDE_ENV_FILE` only) ### Related - #440 — added the CLAUDE_SESSION_ID fallback (CLOSED — the cause of this regression) - #482 — added the recovery procedure to docs/ops.md (OPEN — may become unnecessary once this issue is fixed) - #508 — multi-N WIP loop (CLOSED 2026-05-23 — correctly implemented, but ineffective due to this bug) - #451 — improve the error hint in clarify-intent SKILL.md (OPEN — adjacent topic) - Upstream: anthropics/claude-code#27987 Related to #482 Related to #482


### FEATURE: ops: WIP state recovery procedure when CLAUDE_SESSION_ID propagation fails (2026-05-25, 3e23f64c8d7a088faa3391a833f49ebd1b08db4b, #482)
Background: `bin/github-issues/wip-state.sh set` can silently fail during `/clarify-intent` Completion when neither `$CLAUDE_ENV_FILE` nor `$CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` is available in the Bash subprocess env. The skill is documented to warn and continue (it does not abort the workflow), but the practical effect is that the Projects v2 Status remains `Todo` rather than flipping to `In Progress`, and no fingerprint is written — so subsequent `check` returns `none` and other sessions cannot detect the lock. Confirmed reproduction: session 20260523-102502 working on #444 — `clarify-intent` emitted the warning `[clarify-intent: wip-state set failed — Projects v2 Status not updated (exit 2: CLAUDE_SESSION_ID not resolvable)]`. After completing the planning stages, the issue's Projects v2 Status was still `Todo` even though `intent:clarified` was correctly applied. Root cause is the VS Code Claude Code extension environment failing to propagate `$CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` to Bash subprocesses (Anthropic bug anthropics/claude-code#27987 / locally tracked as #440). This issue is **not** about fixing the propagation bug itself (that is #440 / upstream anthropics/claude-code#27987, both already closed/being-addressed). It is about documenting the **manual recovery procedure** so that a user who notices the WIP flag is missing can repair the state without re-running the workflow. Related: #451 covers updating the in-skill error hint to mention the fallback — this issue covers the docs/ops surface. ## Symptom User observation: issue is being actively worked in a session but `gh issue view <N> --json projectItems --jq '[.projectItems[].status.name]'` returns `["Todo"]` instead of `["In Progress"]`. Search history.md / session warnings for the literal substring `wip-state set failed` or `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID not resolvable` to confirm this is the cause (vs. an unclarified intent). ## Recovery procedure (verified) 1. Locate the Claude Code session id. In VS Code extension environments this is the basename of the active session transcript at `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<session-id>.jsonl` (no extension). The encoded-cwd uses `--` in place of `:\` and `\` (e.g. `c--git-agents`). 2. Run `wip-state.sh set` with the session id inlined:    ```    CLAUDE_SESSION_ID=<session-id> bash "$AGENTS_CONFIG_DIR/bin/github-issues/wip-state.sh" set <N>    ``` 3. Verify with:    ```    gh issue view <N> --json projectItems --jq '[.projectItems[] | {title, status: .status.name}]'    CLAUDE_SESSION_ID=<session-id> bash "$AGENTS_CONFIG_DIR/bin/github-issues/wip-state.sh" check <N>    ```    Expected: Status `In Progress`; `check` prints `same`. ## Why this works `wip-state.sh` already supports `$CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` as a fallback when `$CLAUDE_ENV_FILE` is unreadable (see `resolve_session_id`, `bin/github-issues/wip-state.sh:102`). The inline form bypasses the propagation bug by injecting the variable directly into the child process environment.
Changes: - Add a new `## WIP state recovery` section to `docs/ops.md` containing the 3-step procedure above and a one-line pointer back to this issue. Place near other operational runbooks; do not duplicate into multiple files. - Cross-link: from this section, link out to #440 (root cause) and #451 (error-hint discoverability) so a reader following any chain ends up here for the recovery procedure. - Do **not** modify `wip-state.sh` itself — the propagation bug fix already shipped with #440. This issue is docs/ops only. - Do **not** modify `clarify-intent/SKILL.md` here — that surface belongs to #451. ## Related - #440 — wip-state.sh: VS Code Claude Code fails to resolve session-id due to CLAUDE_ENV_FILE not propagating (CLOSED — added the CLAUDE_SESSION_ID fallback this procedure depends on) - #451 — clarify-intent/SKILL.md: CLAUDE_SESSION_ID fallback not mentioned in session-id error hint (OPEN — sibling, error-hint surface) - Upstream: anthropics/claude-code#27987 Related to #440 Related to #451

Background: /update-docs (Path A, ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on) stages entries into WORKTREE_NOTES.md `## History Notes` / `## Changelog Notes` before /worktree-end appends them to docs/history.md and CHANGELOG.md. In practice, Japanese entries appear in public repo WORKTREE_NOTES.md history/changelog sections frequently, only surfacing as a mismatch at /worktree-end Step 6i. rules/language.md spec: - history.md entries: always English regardless of repo visibility - public repo docs: always English - private repo docs: Japanese allowed (except history.md)

### FEATURE: harness: enforce confirm-plan protocol via hook/code rather than prompt instructions (2026-05-25, ddcd7c4f0e7d194d00e4bc6224f9a61690ee3355, #524)
Background: `skills/_shared/confirm-plan.md` Step 2 specifies that after writing a plan artifact (intent.md / outline.md / detail.md), the **orchestrator MUST NOT emit any path representation** — no markdown link, no fullpath, no `file:///` URI, no relative/tilde path, no path appended to a Japanese sentence. The `show-plan-link.js` PostToolUse hook is the sole authoritative path surface and also routes the artifact to a matching VS Code window (#291). Step 3 specifies that under `CONFIRM_<STEP>=ON`, the orchestrator MUST call `AskUserQuestion` to ask "Proceed with this, or revise?" — not a plain-text question. Parent: #328 (URL/path-presentation-protocol umbrella). ## Observed violations (session 20260524-220446, clarify-intent for #254/#252) Recorded honestly without blame, to capture failure modes that prompt-level instructions did not prevent: 1. **Path representation emission** — after Write of intent.md, the orchestrator output both a markdown link `[20260524-220446-intent.md](...)` and the fullpath as plain text in the same response. Two distinct prohibitions in Step 2 violated simultaneously. 2. **AskUserQuestion bypass** — Step 3 confirmation was issued as a plain-text question ("Approve / Revise") instead of via the `AskUserQuestion` tool, breaking the structured-approval contract. Additionally, the VS Code preview did not open as expected, suggesting the `show-plan-link.js` hook may not have fired or its routing target was ambiguous (a separate `20260524-225717-intent.md` from another session appeared in the IDE instead). ## Hypothesis Prompt-level instructions in SKILL.md / shared protocols are insufficient to prevent these violations reliably. The protocol is correctly written and the LLM had access to it, yet the violations occurred. Stale memory entries (e.g. older `feedback_workflow_plans_link_not_clickable.md` advising fullpath text emission, which predated #291) compound the risk.
Changes: Move the enforcement surface from prompt instructions to code/hook so it is structurally impossible for the orchestrator to violate: 1. **Prevent path emission at the hook layer**: extend `show-plan-link.js` (or add a sibling PostToolUse hook) to inject the breadcrumb as the only path surface. Optionally, add a UserPromptSubmit / Stop hook that scans the assistant's pending response for absolute paths matching `<PLANS_DIR>/.*-(intent|outline|detail)\.md` and strips/warns when the show-plan-link breadcrumb is already present in the same turn. 2. **Force AskUserQuestion via tool gating**: when `CONFIRM_<STEP>=ON` is active and the artifact was just written, a hook could set a turn-scoped marker; the next assistant turn that ends without an `AskUserQuestion` call could be flagged via a Stop hook. 3. **Diagnose show-plan-link.js routing**: confirm the hook fires for every Write to `<PLANS_DIR>/<session-id>-*.md` and routes to the correct VS Code window when multiple sessions are open concurrently (relates to #291 and #486 history). ## Out of scope - Modifying upstream Claude Code permission matcher. - Changing the confirm-plan protocol itself (the protocol is correctly written — the gap is enforcement). ## Refs - `skills/_shared/confirm-plan.md` Steps 2 + 3 - `hooks/show-plan-link.js` (#275, #291, #486) - `hooks/show-diff.js` (PreToolUse diff preview) - Sibling closed issues under #328: #275, #291, #314, #318, #326, #486 - Parent architecture migration: #253

Bundling into the same PR would blur the review focus. - The Step K fixes require confirming consistency with the scope-reduction history of the #222 parent issue, and the decision was made not to expand this session's scope.

### FEATURE: WORKFLOW_OFF / WORKTREE_OFF session markers honored by enforce-worktree.js but ignored by hooks/pre-commit (2026-05-26, 58778007cf0d4fa07636688767e54c4812592448, #550)
Background: The session-scoped escape hatches (`WORKFLOW_OFF` and `WORKTREE_OFF`) are documented as suspending worktree enforcement, but only the Claude Code PreToolUse layer (`hooks/enforce-worktree.js`) honors the marker file. The git pre-commit shell hook (`hooks/pre-commit`) does not check for the marker and continues to block the commit, even when the PreToolUse hook has already passed it through. ### Observed Session 947b8c1e-a1ae-4c0c-a700-b211710c0cb2 (issue #528 finalize). After emitting `<<WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKFLOW_OFF: ...>>` and confirming the marker file existed at `~/.claude/projects/workflow/<sid>.workflow-off`, attempting `git commit` from the main worktree of a sibling repo still produced: ``` ENFORCE_WORKTREE: commits from main worktree are blocked. ``` The error originates from `hooks/pre-commit:125`, not from `enforce-worktree.js`. ### Root cause `hooks/pre-commit` (lines 60–129) recognizes only three bypasses: - `ENFORCE_WORKTREE_EXCLUDE` glob patterns - `COMPOSE_DOC_APPEND_SKILL=1` env (for history.md / CHANGELOG.md commits) - `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=off` config var It does NOT read `~/.claude/projects/workflow/<sid>.workflow-off` or `<sid>.worktree-off` markers. `hooks/enforce-worktree.js:1234` does honor the marker (`isWorkflowOff(sid)` check). ### Documentation mismatch `rules/workflow-off.md` lists a 'What is bypassed' table that includes `enforce-worktree.js` as 'Yes' but omits `hooks/pre-commit` entirely. A user following the docs would expect WORKFLOW_OFF to enable commits, but it does not. ### Scope - Same issue applies to `WORKTREE_OFF` marker (`<sid>.worktree-off`). - Affects multi-repo workflows where a sibling repo needs a quick maintenance commit while another repo's worktree session is active. - Repro: emit `<<WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKFLOW_OFF: testing>>`, verify marker exists, then `git commit` from main worktree on a feature branch → blocked by `hooks/pre-commit:125`.
Changes: Two candidate directions — design decision needed: **A. Make `hooks/pre-commit` honor the marker** — read `~/.claude/projects/workflow/<sid>.workflow-off` (and `.worktree-off`) and skip the main-worktree check when the marker exists. Requires resolving `<sid>` inside the git hook context (env var fallback or transcript scan, same approach as `hooks/lib/workflow-state.js:resolveSessionId`). **B. Document the gap explicitly** — keep pre-commit as the last-line defense and update the `rules/workflow-off.md` bypass table to make clear that pre-commit is NOT bypassed, with the recommended workaround (linked worktree, or `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=off` in agents `.env`). A aligns with the apparent design intent (PreToolUse layer already trusts the marker); B preserves an additional safety net. Decision depends on whether pre-commit is intended as a redundant guard or as the canonical commit-time guard. Related to #241. Related to #241

### FEATURE: enforce-worktree: allow gh issue create/edit from main worktree (2026-05-14, #250)
Background: The dual-write workflow defined in \`rules/github-issues.md\` requires: 1. \`gh issue create\` — gets new issue number #N 2. Append \`- [ ] #N <title>\` to \`docs/todo.md\` — committed to main Step 1 is currently blocked from the main worktree by \`enforce-worktree.js\` because gh CLI commands are write-classified. Step 2 is allowed (todo.md edits from main are permitted). This contradiction forces creating a feature worktree just to file a new issue, which defeats the lightweight dual-write design. \`gh issue create\` / \`gh issue edit\` / \`gh issue comment\` perform writes only to the GitHub side — they do not modify the local working tree. They should be allowed from the main worktree.
Changes: - [ ] \`hooks/enforce-worktree.js\`: classify \`gh issue create|edit|comment|close|reopen|delete\` as read-side for the worktree guard (writes happen on GitHub, not local FS). - [ ] Consider same treatment for \`gh project item-add|item-edit\` since Projects v2 backfill follows the same pattern. - [ ] Keep \`gh pr merge\` / \`gh pr close\` blocked from main as today (these affect local branches via cleanup). - [ ] Tests covering the new exemption set. ## Acceptance - From main worktree: \`gh issue create ...\` succeeds without permission prompt - From main worktree: \`gh pr merge ...\` still blocked (regression guard)


### FEATURE: workflow-gate / enforce-worktree: misclassifies repo when command runs from a linked worktree (2026-05-17, #321)
Background: When a Bash tool command runs from a linked worktree, PreToolUse hooks (`hooks/workflow-gate.js` and `hooks/enforce-worktree.js`) resolve the repo via `process.cwd()` — but the hook process inherits **Claude Code's parent-process cwd, NOT the Bash tool's cwd**. The Bash tool's cwd is the linked worktree; the hook sees Claude Code's cwd (typically the main agents repo on branch `main`). This causes both hooks to evaluate state against the wrong repo: ### workflow-gate.js 1. If main repo has stale staged docs (e.g. `docs/history.md` left from a prior session), the gate flags the commit as `docs-only commit` and demands `user_verification`, even though the worktree's actual staged files are behavior code. 2. After unstaging the main repo's stale files, the gate still resolves to main, sees no staged files there, falls through to `return primary`, and demands `docs + user_verification` — because `isWorktreeContext` returns false for the main repo. ### enforce-worktree.js Blocks `gh issue create` (and other gh writes) with `ENFORCE_WORKTREE: write blocked. Reason: main worktree (branch 'main')` — even when the Bash tool's cwd is the linked worktree on a feature branch. Filing this very issue was blocked by this bug; user had to toggle `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=off` to file it. ## Reproduction (encountered live during chore/workflow-prompts-cleanup) 1. Worktree `C:/git/worktrees/workflow-prompts-cleanup/agents` has 3 staged files (CLAUDE.md / detail-reviewer.md / bin/review-skill-size) on branch `chore/workflow-prompts-cleanup`. 2. Main agents repo has `docs/history.md` staged (stale). 3. Bash tool cwd = worktree. `git commit` invoked. 4. Hook reports `docs-only commit` and demands `user_verification`. 5. After `git -C C:/git/agents restore --staged docs/history.md`, hook reports `docs, user_verification` missing. 6. Attempting `gh issue create` to file this very report: `enforce-worktree.js` blocks with `main worktree (branch 'main')`. ## Root cause - Hooks invoked by Claude Code's PreToolUse subsystem inherit Claude Code's parent-process cwd, NOT the Bash tool's cwd. - `resolveRepoDir` (workflow-gate.js) uses `process.cwd()` as `primary`; this resolves to Claude Code's cwd whenever `-C` is absent and `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` is unset. - `startDir = cArg || process.cwd()` (enforce-worktree.js line 909) suffers the same fallback. `gh` writes have no `-C` arg, so the fallback always fires. - Hook input JSON contains `tool_input.command` only — no cwd field. ## Workarounds (live-tested) - Git commands: pass `-C <worktree-path>` explicitly. - Set `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR=<worktree-path>` — works for workflow-gate, **NOT** for enforce-worktree (it uses `process.cwd()` directly without CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR fallback). - For `gh` writes: toggle `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=off` (this issue itself was filed using this workaround). ## Suggested fix Either: - Have Claude Code pass the Bash tool's cwd in the hook input JSON (e.g. `tool_input.cwd`), and have all PreToolUse hooks prefer it over `process.cwd()`. - Or have both hooks scan all linked worktrees of every known repo and prefer the one matching the bash command's intent (e.g. the worktree with staged changes when primary has none). ## Notes - Group/parent: #296 (Group: tracking: worktree-end x CWD issues).
Changes: When a Bash tool command runs from a linked worktree, PreToolUse hooks (`hooks/workflow-gate.js` and `hooks/enforce-worktree.js`) resolve the repo via `process.cwd()` — but the hook process inherits


### FEATURE: worktree-end: orphan worktree directory cleanup gap on Windows CWD lock (2026-05-17, #322)
Background: In `/worktree-end`'s final cleanup, when `git worktree remove` suffers a partial failure due to Windows' OS-level CWD lock, there is no authorized path for Claude Code to delete the physical directory. Actually occurred at the end of the #308 (Content Date backfill) work: `gh pr merge --delete-branch` → local branch deletion failed → `git worktree remove` returned "Permission denied" → after `ExitWorktree action: keep` the worktree was deregistered but the physical directory remained → the user deleted it manually. Related: #317 (WORKTREE_NOTES.md integration on the worktree-start side). Both are worktree lifecycle improvements, but the target skill/stage/file are independent. ## Root causes 1. **OS-level CWD lock**: when the CC process's CWD is inside the linked worktree, `git worktree remove` cannot complete atomically (Windows only). 2. **Non-transactional nature of `git worktree remove`**: metadata deletion happens first, and if file deletion fails there is no rollback. 3. **`enforce-worktree.js` blocks `Remove-Item` / `rm -rf` from the main worktree**: correct as a safety measure, but no legitimate path exists for deleting an orphaned directory. 4. **`cleanup-orphan-dir.js` rejects non-empty directories**: unusable while remnants exist. 5. **No escape hatch**: there is no authorized path that can get through all of the above. ## Proposed fixes (needs discussion) - **A**: Add `--force-if-not-registered` to `cleanup-orphan-dir.js` — allow recursive deletion only for paths not included in `git worktree list`. - **B**: Tighten the behavior of `ExitWorktree action: keep` — add a guarantee that the git worktree registration is preserved (currently suspected to have been deregistered in the #308 case). - **C**: Build a CWD-lock release step into the `/worktree-end` procedure — return CC's CWD to the main worktree before deletion. A has the highest independence and the smallest blast radius. B/C need side-effect investigation. ## Acceptance criteria - In cases where `git worktree remove` suffers a partial failure inside a linked worktree, CC can complete cleanup without user intervention - Safety: the existing protection in `enforce-worktree.js` (not accidentally deleting the main worktree or a registered worktree) is preserved ## Reference - Orphan directory that occurred at #308 close: `C:/git/worktrees/content-date/` (deleted manually by the user) - See the session history at #308 close for detailed root cause analysis
Changes: In `/worktree-end`'s final cleanup, when `git worktree remove` suffers a partial failure due to Windows' OS-level CWD lock, there is no authorized path for Claude Code to delete the physical directory

### FEATURE: ISSUE_CLOSE_SKILL=1 bypass should also cover the Phase 2 docs(history) upload-to-remote step from main (2026-05-21, #280)
Background: Originally filed (2026-05-19) as: '/issue-close leaves docs/history.md staged in main worktree without committing.' The commit-from-main part was resolved by PR #399 (#325): Phase 2 /issue-close-finalize now performs Step E (doc-append + staging + committing) from the main worktree via the new three-axis AND bypass `isAllowedHistoryWriteViaIssueCloseSkill` in `hooks/enforce-worktree.js`. ## Remaining gap The bypass covers staging and committing of `docs/history.md` / `docs/history/` but does NOT cover the upload-to-remote step. After the Phase 2 commit lands on main, sending it to origin is still blocked by `enforce-worktree.js` when run from the main worktree under `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on`. Observed during #325 Phase 2: the user had to emit `WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKTREE_OFF` (session-scoped escape hatch) to upload the `docs(history): record issue #325` commit (`0e55afd`) to origin. ## Desired behavior Extend `isAllowedHistoryWriteViaIssueCloseSkill` (or add a sibling allow predicate) so that the canonical Phase 2 upload of a `docs(history): record issue #N` commit on main also passes the bypass when prefixed with `ISSUE_CLOSE_SKILL=1`. Constraints: - Only authorize the upload when HEAD is a single commit whose message matches `docs(history): record issue #<digits>` and whose tree only changes `docs/history.md` / `docs/history/`. - Branch must be the default branch (e.g. `main`). - `ISSUE_CLOSE_SKILL=1` inline prefix required (same convention as the staging/commit bypass). ## Out of scope - Loosening `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on` defaults. - Allowing arbitrary uploads from main. - Removing the session-scoped escape hatch as a fallback. ## Related - #325 (introduced the centralized Phase 2 write; surfaced this remaining gap) - PR #399 (added the existing three-axis AND bypass)
Changes: Originally filed (2026-05-19) as: '/issue-close leaves docs/history.md staged in main worktree without committing.' The commit-from-main part was resolved by PR #399 (#325): Phase 2 /issue-close-final

### FEATURE: enforce-worktree: posix-redirect false-positive — stderr-to-devnull inside command substitution misclassified as write (2026-05-23, #359)
Background: enforce-worktree: posix-redirect false-positive — stderr-to-devnull inside command substitution misclassified as write
Changes: enforce-worktree blocks read-only commands that combine for-loop + command substitution + stderr redirect to /dev/null.


### FEATURE: enforce-worktree: non-Group-A commands false-positive on quoted >/redirect chars (2026-05-23, #460)
Background: #371 added processing that strips heredoc/quoted bodies before passing to classify() for Group A gh/pr/issue/repo create|edit|close|comment commands. However, `>` `<` `>>` etc. contained in quoted strings inside generic-command arguments (grep/sed/awk, etc.) are still misjudged as writes via posix-redirect. Repro: ``` grep -nE "foo > bar" file.txt   # → judged write (expected: read) grep -nE "a>b" file.txt          # → judged write (expected: read) ``` ### Root cause - `stripQuotedArgs` correctly strips regex content (converts to `grep -nE "" file.txt`) - However, `STRIP_KINDS = new Set(["file-op"])` in `hooks/lib/bash-write-patterns.js` means only file-op patterns look at the stripped cmd - Since `posix-redirect` is not included in `STRIP_KINDS`, it checks for `>` against the **original cmd** → matches the `>` inside the quoted regex → judged as write ### Impact - Cases where a generic command's quoted regex/pattern contains `>` etc. get blocked from the main worktree - Corresponds to observation (2) of the two observations reported in #369 (observation (1) was already fixed in #371) - Explicitly excluded from #371's scope (Group A) (intent.md: "commands other than Group A retain current behavior") - Filing this very issue was itself blocked by this bug (the `>` in the body argument inside `bash issue-create-dispatch.sh` was misjudged)
Changes: - Add `posix-redirect` to `STRIP_KINDS`, or otherwise revise `hooks/lib/bash-write-patterns.js` so posix-redirect is also tested against the `stripQuotedArgs` result - Add test cases (around tests/feature-bash-write-patterns.sh) covering quoted redirect/regex in generic commands - Scope: the classify() logic in `hooks/lib/bash-write-patterns.js`, related tests Related to: - #369 (parent incident; observation (1) already fixed in #371, observation (2) is this issue) - #371 (Group A fix, merged via PR #452) - #359 (sibling: posix-redirect false-positive in command substitution `$(... 2>/dev/null)`)
