### FEATURE: workflow-gate: block premature WORKFLOW_USER_VERIFIED emit under ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on (2026-05-27, #577)
Background: When `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on`, CLAUDE.md Step 8 says "No action here" for the user verification step. However, Claude sometimes emits `<<WORKFLOW_USER_VERIFIED: reason>>` before the first commit (i.e., before a PR exists for the branch), violating the intended defer-to-worktree-end protocol. This is a sub-issue of #225 (commit gate chicken-and-egg design contradiction) surfaced during triage: the prompting was ambiguous and there was no hook-level enforcement.
Changes: - **Hook (workflow-gate.js)**: Add `hasOpenPrForBranch(repoDir)` helper (fail-open on gh errors). Block `<<WORKFLOW_USER_VERIFIED>>` emission when `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on` + `isWorktreeContext=true` + `hasOpenPrForBranch=false`. Error message directs user to defer to `/worktree-end` Step 4 or use `WORKFLOW_OFF` for emergency bypass. - **SKILL_MAP (workflow-gate.js line ~539)**: Update `user_verification` message to include the premature-emit restriction under `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on`. - **CLAUDE.md Step 8**: Append "Do NOT emit `<<WORKFLOW_USER_VERIFIED>>` here when `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on`" to the existing no-action line. - **skills/_shared/user-verified.md**: Add Notes entry explaining ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on premature emit block.


### FEATURE: ISSUE_CREATE_PROJECT_NUM default of 1 is hardcoded for agents, causing automatic Project v2 attach to fail in other repos (2026-05-30, #641)
Background: Issues created via `/issue-create` are designed to be automatically attached to Projects v2 by `bin/github-issues/issue-create.sh`, but the default project number is hardcoded to `1`, which is the agents repo's project. ``` # bin/github-issues/issue-create.sh:17 PROJECT_NUM="${ISSUE_CREATE_PROJECT_NUM:-1}" ``` In my-private-repo, Project v2 is #2 (`PVT_kwHOAMF_jc4BYZ4b` / "my-private-repo — Issue Timeline"), and running `/issue-create` without `ISSUE_CREATE_PROJECT_NUM=2` set in the environment does not attach to #2 (it gets mis-attached to agents Project #1 instead). Actual impact: post-migration issues in my-private-repo (#41, #42, #44) are missing from my-private-repo's Project v2 and fall out of the Project view's listing management. The migration itself (Steps 1–5) is complete (all 38 items #3–#40 attached correctly). Design issue (`rules/core-principles.md` §1 SSOT): - The mapping between repo and Project v2 is not established as SSOT — mis-attachment occurs unless `ISSUE_CREATE_PROJECT_NUM` is manually set in each repo's `.env` - Because the default value is hardcoded to agents, silent failure is likely in setups that handle multiple repos
Changes: Short-term fix (my-private-repo side): - Add `ISSUE_CREATE_PROJECT_NUM=2` to `agents/.env` - Manually correct un-attached issues (#41, #42, #44) via `gh project item-add 2 --owner nirecom --url <url>` Medium-term improvement (this file): - Remove the default value for `PROJECT_NUM` → warn and skip when unset, or introduce a mechanism to resolve the Project from the repo name - Strengthen the warning on attach failure (explicitly state which project number the attach attempt targeted)


### FEATURE: fix(issue-close-finalize): Step E fails with E4 on Windows (arg-list-too-long in github-contents-write.sh) (2026-06-03, #706)
Background: `/issue-close-finalize` Step E writes `docs/history.md` via `bin/lib/github-contents-write.sh`. On Windows with long --background/--changes args, the shell command exceeds the OS arg-list limit, causing exit code E4 (arg-list-too-long). Observed in session 20260531-211603 (#696 close): the history.md entry was not written; manual `doc-append` + commit + push was required to recover.
Changes: Rewrite `github-contents-write.sh` (or its callers) to pass long text arguments via a temp file or stdin rather than as positional shell arguments, eliminating the Windows arg-list-too-long failure path.


### FEATURE: confirm-plan: plan content stays invisible while the orchestrator keeps issuing approval AskUserQuestion (2026-06-04, #583)
Background: The confirm-plan protocol (`skills/_shared/confirm-plan.md`, `make-outline-plan/SKILL.md` rules section) delegates displaying the plan file's path entirely to the `show-plan-link.js` PostToolUse hook, and the orchestrator must not print the path itself (enforced by the `stop-confirm-plan-guard.js` Stop hook). However, in cases where `show-plan-link.js` does not fire (e.g. #574 — `assemble-mandatory.sh` writes outline.md via the Bash tool, so the Write PostToolUse hook never triggers), neither the plan content nor the link ever reaches the user. Even so, the orchestrator keeps issuing an AskUserQuestion saying "please review the content," leaving the user with no idea what to review. ### Reproduction in this session (20260526-223459) What prompts/hooks the orchestrator followed and how it behaved: 1. `assemble-mandatory.sh` writes `outline.md` via the Bash tool    → `show-plan-link.js` never fires (Write hook does not trigger on Bash PostToolUse — same root cause as #574) 2. The orchestrator follows the confirm-plan instruction in `make-outline-plan/SKILL.md` rules section Step 8 and issues an AskUserQuestion:    > "Please review the content of outline.md. You can preview it from the link in the addendum."    Underlying prompt: `skills/make-outline-plan/SKILL.md` rules — "NO path output — `show-plan-link.js` PostToolUse hook emits the sole authoritative breadcrumb. Orchestrator MUST NOT print, duplicate, translate, paraphrase, or reformat the path."    → Because `show-plan-link.js` is the "sole authoritative" source, the orchestrator cannot emit the link itself 3. User response: "Where is this link in the addendum?" → it becomes clear the link never arrived 4. The orchestrator writes the path directly    → the `stop-confirm-plan-guard.js` Stop hook blocks it:    > "[confirm-plan] Step 2 violation: orchestrator emitted a `~/.workflow-plans/` path representation."    Underlying hook: `hooks/stop-confirm-plan-guard.js` — a rule also recorded in memory `feedback_confirm_plan_no_path_emission.md` 5. The orchestrator issues the same AskUserQuestion again without the path (content still invisible)    → the user points out, "Did you display it somewhere and just assume you had?" 6. Eventually the workaround of pasting the full content directly into chat was used, and the user approved it ### Structure of the problem - The design of `stop-confirm-plan-guard.js` and `show-plan-link.js` depends on the premise that "the Write tool writes the plan file" - Writes via `assemble-mandatory.sh` through Bash do not satisfy that premise - Result: the orchestrator falls into a deadlock of "must not print the path" vs. "but the hook never fired either," repeating a loop of requesting approval while the content stays invisible - This problem will structurally recur until #574 is fixed
Changes: Address via one of the following (or a combination): - **Option A (fix the orchestrator-side prompt)**: Add an instruction to `make-outline-plan/SKILL.md` Step 4a / Step 8 —   "After assemble-mandatory.sh, touch-write `<session-id>-outline.md` via the Write tool (content unchanged) to force show-plan-link.js to fire." - **Option B (fix the confirm-plan protocol)**: Add an explicit rule to `skills/_shared/confirm-plan.md` —   "Present the plan body inline in chat before issuing the approval AskUserQuestion (may be omitted if show-plan-link.js has already fired)." - **Option C (root fix: address #574 first)**: Make writes via `assemble-mandatory.sh` equivalent to a Write tool call. If #574 is resolved first, this issue may be automatically resolved as well. Related to #574


### FEATURE: fix(enforce-worktree): gh api -X PATCH blocked from linked worktree — cannot determine repo root (2026-06-04, #639)
Background: `issue-close-stage` Step F promotes the sentinel via: ```bash gh api -X PATCH "repos/<owner/repo>/issues/comments/$COMMENT_ID" -f body="<!-- issue-close-sentinel: appended -->" ``` This is a sanctioned operation (part of the Phase 1 close path), but `enforce-worktree.js` blocks it with: > ENFORCE_WORKTREE: gh write blocked. Reason: cannot determine repo root for this command. ## Root cause `enforce-worktree.js` identifies the repo for `gh` commands by their subcommand/argument structure. For `gh api -X PATCH repos/owner/repo/issues/comments/N` it cannot extract the local repo root, so it falls into the blocked-by-default path.
Changes: `issue-close-stage` Step F promotes the sentinel via:


### FEATURE: update-docs stages History Notes that are never consumed in issue-backed sessions (skip_history dead-path) (2026-06-04, #652)
Background: When `closes_issues` is non-empty, `/worktree-end` Step 6h passes `--skip-history` to `compose-doc-append-entry`, so `## History Notes` bullets staged by `/update-docs` into `WORKTREE_NOTES.md` are silently discarded. `docs/history.md` is instead written by Phase 2 Step E (`/issue-close-finalize`). The two paths are mutually exclusive, but `/update-docs` has no visibility into which path will run — it always stages History Notes even when they will be dropped. Every issue-backed session with `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on` thus performs wasted staging work. If Phase 2 Step E fails or is skipped, no history entry is written at all.
Changes: Propose one of: 1. `/update-docs` detects `closes_issues` is non-empty and skips History Notes staging (with a note to the user explaining that `issue-close-finalize` Step E handles `history.md` instead). 2. The `## History Notes` section in `WORKTREE_NOTES.md` serves as a fallback: `compose-doc-append-entry` consumes it only when `closes_issues` is empty OR Phase 2 Step E did not run. References: `skills/worktree-end/SKILL.md` Step 6h (`--skip-history` flag); `bin/github-issues/step-e.sh` (Phase 2 history.md writer).


### FEATURE: enforce-worktree: unblock compose-doc-append gh api write from the main worktree (#672 followup) (2026-06-04, #691)
Background: In #672, the `COMPOSE_DOC_APPEND_SKILL=1` bypass was removed, but running `compose-doc-append-entry` from the main worktree in `/worktree-end` Step 6h is now blocked by enforce-worktree.js. Confirmed blocks: 1. The `gh api -X PUT` call made by `compose-doc-append-entry` is blocked in the main worktree. In the code, if `isGhWriteCommand(cmd)` is true it should reach `done()` (allow) after the session-scope check, but it was blocked in practice (the flow needs further investigation) 2. `classify()` also returns "write" when a command argument contains git-write strings, e.g. `grep -n "git push\|git commit" path/to/file` — `stripQuotedArgs` may be failing to strip it Current workaround: use the `<<WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKTREE_OFF: ...>>` sentinel (already done in #687).
Changes: - Identify and fix the actual block path in enforce-worktree.js:   - Fix the gh write command handling (isGhWriteCommand path) so it is correctly allowed from the main worktree, or   - Reorder so the gh write session-scope allow definitely runs before the main-checkout block - Fix the false positive in bash-write-patterns.js's `classify()` for git-write strings contained in command arguments to grep/rg etc. (missing quoted-args stripping) - Add regression tests Related to #690 (parent issue: unifying the issue-close/docs-write workflow)


### FEATURE: issue-to-history.sh: resolve date as max(closedAt, merge_commit_date) to avoid doc-append hard-block (2026-06-04, #693)
Background: issue-to-history.sh passes the issue's closedAt as --date to doc-append. doc-append.py hard-blocks any new entry whose date precedes the current max date in history.md (ascending order invariant), so Phase 2 Step E fails when an issue is manually closed before the PR merges. Reproduced in the #672 session (2026-05-30/31): #672 was manually closed at 2026-05-30T14:25:00Z but history.md's last entry was 2026-05-31 (#673). Step E failed with failed-E1 and the entries for #672/#600/#527 had to be backfilled via in-session reconcile, adding hours to the session.
Changes: - In issue-to-history.sh (or step-e.sh), resolve the entry date as max(closedAt, merge_commit_date) rather than closedAt alone. - merge_commit_date is already available as MERGE_COMMIT in step-e.sh; git log --format=%cs "$MERGE_COMMIT" yields the date. - Alternatively add a --date YYYY-MM-DD override flag to issue-to-history.sh so step-e.sh can pass the resolved date explicitly. Related to #694, #695


### FEATURE: session-close Final Report: surface /issue-reconcile guidance when historyEntry is failed-E* (2026-06-04, #694)
Background: When Phase 2 Step E fails (historyEntry: failed-E1 or failed-E2 etc.), the Final Report shows the error code in the Closed Issue Outcomes table but gives no instruction on what to do next. The user has to interpret the code themselves and remember to run /issue-reconcile. Reproduced in the #672 session: historyEntry showed failed-E1 for #672 but the Final Report gave no recovery guidance. The user had to ask what impact this had. The manual reconcile (doc-append + commit + push) added hours to the session.
Changes: - In worktree-final-report.js (or its template), when any issue entry has historyEntry matching /^failed-/, append a recovery notice to the Closed Issue Outcomes section: 'History entry missing — run /issue-reconcile to backfill docs/history.md.' - The notice should name the specific issue numbers affected so the user knows the scope. Related to #693, #695


### FEATURE: fix: docs/history.md left in M state after Phase 2 issue-close-finalize writes via GitHub Contents API (2026-06-04, #707)
Background: After `/issue-close-finalize` Step E writes `docs/history.md` via the GitHub Contents API (#672), the local working tree is not updated. If the previous local content differed from what the API wrote (e.g. a prior session left a pre-written entry), the file appears as `M` (modified) in `git status` until the next `git pull`. This causes `git pull --ff-only` in `/worktree-end` Step 6g to fail with a dirty-working-tree error. Observed: session 20260531-211603 started with `M docs/history.md` — a #685 entry written locally by a previous session that was identical to the already-merged remote commit `607f4fc`. Required manual `git checkout -- docs/history.md` before pull.
Changes: After Step E succeeds via GitHub Contents API, explicitly refresh the local file to match remote: either `git checkout -- docs/history.md` (if working tree is clean otherwise) or record the file as a known-dirty path and add a checkout step to `/worktree-end` Step 6g pre-flight.


### FEATURE: feat: supervisor finding schema — add structured environment context fields (cwd, git_root_resolved) (2026-06-16, #886)
Background: The `detail` field of a Layer 1 finding is free text only. For Layer 2 to obtain environment context (CWD, whether git root resolution succeeded), it must parse the natural-language text, which makes programmatic correlation analysis difficult. This is complementary to #885 (adding a `reason` tag to enforce-worktree.js).
Changes: Add an optional `context` object field to the supervisor finding schema. - `cwd` (string): the CWD at block time - `git_root_resolved` (boolean): whether `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` succeeded Populate it from `enforce-worktree.js` and `workflow-gate.js`. Existing findings without a `context` field are treated as backward compatible with `git_root_resolved: true`.


### FEATURE: feat: enforce-worktree.js — add a co_blocked_by field to findings when multiple hooks block the same command (2026-06-16, #887)
Background: When the same command is blocked by both `enforce-worktree.js` and `enforce-issue-close.js` (a double-block), two independent findings are currently recorded. Co-occurrence is a signal of an upstream (environmental) cause, but Layer 2 can only detect it by manually correlating the two findings. In the c6ede23d session, `bash -c "ISSUE_CLOSE_SKILL=1 gh issue close 853..."` was blocked by both hooks simultaneously, but the supervisor analyzed each finding independently.
Changes: When `enforce-worktree.js` detects a command that would also be blocked by `enforce-issue-close.js` (contains `gh issue close` and does not match `INLINE_SKILL_RE`), attach a `"co_blocked_by": ["enforce-issue-close"]` field to the finding. Layer 2 can use this structured signal to speed up upstream-cause determination. Combined with the `reason` tag from #885, the causal chain — "an orphaned CWD triggered enforce-worktree, which then cascade-blocked enforce-issue-close as well" — becomes readable from the finding data alone.


### FEATURE: feat: supervisor Layer 2 §5 End-to-End Integrity — clarify the causal-chain tracing requirement on cascade failures (2026-06-16, #889)
Background: `agents/supervisor.md` Layer 2 JD Checklist §5 ("Perspective — integrity-preserving end-to-end") checks whether a change preserves integrity across the whole pipeline. It does not explicitly require tracing the causal chain to identify a single most-upstream root cause when a series of blocks (a cascade) is observed. In the c6ede23d session review, §5 remained limited to "confirming" end-to-end integrity and did not lead to tracing the most-upstream cause of the block cascade.
Changes: Expand the wording of `agents/supervisor.md` §5: "When a series of block operations is observed (multiple failures within the same session phase), trace the causal chain back to a single most-upstream root cause before filing individual findings. File one root-cause finding at the appropriate severity for the most-upstream cause, and have downstream findings reference it." This works together with #888 (the co-block correlation step).


### FEATURE: feat: supervisor Layer 2 — add timestamp-proximity grouping of Layer 1 findings as an analysis preprocessing step (2026-06-16, #890)
Background: Layer 1 findings are presented to Layer 2 as a flat list. Findings that occur in the same time window often share the same operation attempt, the same session phase, and the same failure mode, but without temporal grouping they tend to be analyzed individually. In the c6ede23d session, 7 enforce-issue-close blocks occurred within 60 seconds around 17:00:22, and each was analyzed as if it had an independent root cause.
Changes: Add a preprocessing directive to the "Inputs to read" section of `agents/supervisor.md`: "Before applying the JD checklist to Layer 1 findings, group them by timestamp proximity (a 60-second window). Findings involving the same hook type or the same Bash command text within the same window are candidates for shared-root-cause analysis. Summarize the cluster as a single environmental observation before analyzing individual findings." Combined with #888 (co-block correlation), this improves analysis coverage.


### FEATURE: Workflow State Machine: session ID injection and state inheritance across VS Code restarts (2026-06-20, #94)
Background: When VS Code restarts (on hook changes, shutdown, etc.), a new session_id is issued and the old session workflow state file becomes orphaned, causing workflow-gate to treat all steps as pending and block commits.
Changes: Resolved by three combined changes. (1) session-start.js: added inheritance logic — on new session start, scans ~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<session_id>.jsonl files by mtime descending (up to 10), searches stdout of SessionStart/PostCompact attachment entries for 'Current workflow session_id: <uuid>'. If a state with the same cwd+branch exists and is not all-pending, copies its steps. (2) session-start.js: changed output from {} to additionalContext JSON (session_id + state path) to record in transcript for future sessions to reference. (3) post-compact.js (new PostCompact hook): re-injects session_id after compaction to preserve transcript markers. workflow-state.js: added getCurrentContext() and findLatestStateForContext(ctx), added optional ctx (cwd/git_branch) argument to createInitialState(). settings.json: registered PostCompact hook. Tests: tests/feature-workflow-inherit-state.sh (A1-A12, T-C1-T-C3, 15 cases) and tests/feature-workflow-post-compact.sh (B1-B3, 3 cases), 18 cases total, all PASS.


### FEATURE: Relax file-copy restrictions in worktree-start (B-Hybrid Phase 1 implemented) (2026-06-20, #237)
Background: Relax file-copy restrictions in worktree-start (B-Hybrid Phase 1 implemented)
Changes: Implemented main → worktree copying of `.env` / `.private-info-allowlist` and similar files via `bin/worktree-copy-include.js` + `.worktreeinclude` / `.worktreecopyexclude`. Phase 2 (B-Pure) is tracked as a separate entry.


### FEATURE: issue-close-write-outcome.js: refactor to remove --out-file and add to KNOWN_PLANS_DIR_WRITERS (2026-06-22, #1046)
Background: During session `0212867d-869f-4a47-b941-bbe215983a8e` (PR #1042 closing #983 / #878), Step ICF-K of `/issue-close-finalize` needed to write the outcome JSON to `~/.workflow-plans/<session-id>-issue-close-outcome.json` via `bin/issue-close-write-outcome.js`. The script was not in the `KNOWN_PLANS_DIR_WRITERS` allowlist in `hooks/enforce-worktree/main-worktree-allows/plans-dir.js`, so the worker fell back to a `node -e writeFileSync` literal-path pattern — the exact anti-pattern #957 was meant to eliminate by establishing the allowlist. Orthogonality gap (core-principles §4): every sanctioned plans-dir writer should be on the allowlist, but `issue-close-write-outcome.js` was missed when #957 landed. Codex caveat (logged in supervisor-state.json idx=3): a naive add-to-allowlist fix is unsafe because the script currently accepts `--out-file <path>` (and `--non-github` / `--fallback` variants), so plans-dir confinement is not guaranteed by the script itself.
Changes: Two-step fix (preferred): 1. Refactor `bin/issue-close-write-outcome.js` to remove the `--out-file <path>` argument. Resolve the destination internally via `resolvePlansDir() + sessionId`, mirroring `bin/supervisor-write-layer2`'s pattern. Drop the `--non-github` / `--fallback` path-injection variants in favor of internal mode flags. 2. Add `bin/issue-close-write-outcome.js` to `KNOWN_PLANS_DIR_WRITERS` in `hooks/enforce-worktree/main-worktree-allows/plans-dir.js`. Update callers in `skills/issue-close-finalize/SKILL.md`, `skills/session-close/SKILL.md`, and `agents/issue-close-finalize-worker.md` to drop the `--out-file` / `outcome_file_path` argument. Alternative (rejected): extend the allowlist matcher to validate that the `--out-file` argument resolves under plans-dir. Less preferred — opens an argument-injection surface and duplicates path-validation logic that already exists in `resolvePlansDir()`. Related to #957. Related to #957


### FEATURE: fix(workflow-gate): pre_final_report_gate in VALID_STEPS but not SKIPPABLE_STEPS — always blocks pre-commit until manually emitted (2026-06-24, #926)
Background: PR #899 added `pre_final_report_gate` to `VALID_STEPS` in `hooks/lib/workflow-state/state-io.js`. However: - It is NOT in `SKIPPABLE_STEPS` (`["clarify_intent", "research", "outline", "detail", "write_tests", "review_tests", "review_security", "cleanup"]`) - It has no evidence bypass in `hooks/workflow-gate.js` - The sentinel `<<WORKFLOW_MARK_STEP_pre_final_report_gate_complete>>` is emitted only in `skills/session-close/SKILL.md` SC-3.6 — which runs **after** PR merge, i.e. post-commit Result: every session's pre-commit workflow gate check finds `pre_final_report_gate` pending (never emitted pre-commit) and blocks the commit. The gate is structurally impossible to satisfy before the first `/session-close` runs in a CC session. Confirmed in session 20260617-013744 (#897): manually emitting the sentinel was required to unblock the commit. Reported by L2 supervisor (session aa254ce1-23d8-4378-ae91-6c04a67328ba, 2026-06-17T12:40:16Z).
Changes: Fix the gate/emission timing mismatch. Options (pick one): - **(a) Add to SKIPPABLE_STEPS**: `pre_final_report_gate` behaves like `cleanup` — skippable by default, no block before commit. Gate only fires for sessions where evidence is explicitly required. - **(b) Move emission pre-commit**: emit `<<WORKFLOW_MARK_STEP_pre_final_report_gate_complete>>` inside `/worktree-end` Step WE-11 (after env JSON is written) rather than in `session-close` SC-3.6. - **(c) Add evidence bypass**: when `<session-id>-final-report-env.json` exists, treat the gate as complete (analogous to how `write_tests` can be bypassed by the not-needed sentinel). Scope: `hooks/lib/workflow-state/state-io.js` (SKIPPABLE_STEPS), `hooks/workflow-gate.js` (evidence bypass), or `skills/session-close/SKILL.md` + `skills/worktree-end/SKILL.md` (emission timing).


### FEATURE: PG-H1/PG-H2 FAIL: settings.json is missing allow entries for WORKFLOW_PREMISE_FAIL/ACK (2026-06-24, #930)
Background: The PG-H1/PG-H2 tests in `tests/feature-premise-gate.sh` are FAILing. `settings.json` lacks allow entries for the `WORKFLOW_PREMISE_FAIL` and `WORKFLOW_PREMISE_ACK` sentinels, so the hook does not let the sentinels through and the premise gate flow does not work correctly. Detected as a pre-existing bug during the #898 session.
Changes: Add allow entries for `WORKFLOW_PREMISE_FAIL` and `WORKFLOW_PREMISE_ACK` to `settings.json` so that `feature-premise-gate.sh` PG-H1/PG-H2 PASS.


### FEATURE: PG-E1/E2 FAIL: workflow-mark.js returns reject via additionalContext instead of hook_decision (2026-06-24, #931)
Background: The PG-E1/PG-E2 tests in `tests/feature-premise-gate.sh` are FAILing. `hooks/workflow-mark.js` returns the sentinel's reject decision in `additionalContext` instead of the `hook_decision` field, so Claude Code cannot correctly recognize the hook's block decision. It may believe it is blocking while the sentinel actually passes through. Detected as a pre-existing bug during the #898 session.
Changes: Fix the reject path in `hooks/workflow-mark.js` to correctly return `hook_decision: 'block'`, so that `feature-premise-gate.sh` PG-E1/PG-E2 PASS.


### FEATURE: workflow-gate: concurrent sessions sharing CC UUID cause post-reset state collision (2026-06-25, #1082)
Background: #1053 fixed state contamination caused by sequential reuse of the CC UUID (#1072: reset downstream steps to pending when workflow_init completes). However, in environments where the CC UUID is shared across **concurrent** sessions within the same VS Code window, another contamination path remains. Actually observed state (CC UUID 0ad94fb1, 2026-06-24): | step | status | updated_at | |---|---|---| | workflow_init | complete | 13:38:54Z (Session A — the #721 session) | | run_tests | complete | **13:12:40Z (Session B — a different task)** | | research | pending | null | Sequence: 1. Session B (a different task) marks run_tests as complete 2. Session A (this #721 session) completes workflow_init → the #1072 fix resets downstream steps → `run_tests` should return to pending, but... 3. Session B marks run_tests complete again (22:12 JST, after Session A's reset) 4. The oracle detects the inconsistency of `run_tests complete / research pending` and ABORTs The #1072 fix correctly handles sequential reuse, but concurrent write races are out of its scope. Related to #924 (the sequential wsid transition case).
Changes: Scope the state file per "workflow execution instance" rather than per CC UUID. Candidate approaches: - Use the workflow session ID (YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS or UUID) as the filename (e.g. `<wsid>.json`) - Two layers of CC UUID + wsid: CC UUID as the directory, wsid as the filename - Give the state file a `wsid` field, and have the oracle / mark-step-handler skip reading it when the wsid does not match Any of these approaches requires changes to `bin/workflow/next-step` / `hooks/workflow-mark/` / `hooks/lib/workflow-state/`. Related to #924

Background: `hooks/lib/workflow-state.js` is 517 lines (was 528 before PR #856 / fix/842-confirm-hint reduced it by 11 lines). The HARD limit per `rules/coding/file-split.md` is 500 lines. `review-code-size` blocks on this file whenever it appears in a diff.

Keep `workflow-state.js` as a dispatch/re-export entrypoint with no inline logic.


### FEATURE: after worktree-start completes, the next skill does not auto-launch even though no user input is needed (2026-06-28, #1122)
Background: When `/worktree-start` completes normally with `CONFIRM_WORKTREE=OFF` and `status: complete`, even though there is no question for the user and nothing urgent to report, the orchestrator stops after presenting "The next step is `/write-tests`." The next skill does not launch until the user manually sends a prompt. Conditions under which this occurs: - `CONFIRM_WORKTREE=OFF` (no confirmation dialog needed) - `status: complete` or `status: partial` (non-blocking) - No Stop-hook block - No AskUserQuestion needed There is no reason to stop in this state — it is pure UX friction. In the case the user reported, `/write-tests` did not auto-launch after `/worktree-start fix-write-tests-evidence` completed. Related: #479 (the same class of "should auto-continue but stops" problem also occurs at worktree-end Step 5).
Changes: Address via one of the following (details to be decided in the detail plan): **Option A — auto-continue on the worktree-start side:** When `CONFIRM_WORKTREE=OFF` and `status: complete`, call `node "$AGENTS_CONFIG_DIR/bin/workflow/next-step"` on worktree-start completion, and auto-launch the next skill when `ACTION=invoke`. **Option B — extend the supervisor alert's C3 detection:** In addition to C3 off-proposal detection (detecting a proposed `WORKTREE_OFF`), detect the state "Stopped without an AskUserQuestion or an error, yet the oracle has a next step" as a new C3 sub-pattern, and show the user guidance encouraging continuation. **Option C — add a common "auto-chain after skill completion" pattern to skills:** Add a standard auto-chain clause (check oracle → auto-launch next skill) to the Completion section of `SKILL.md`, and share it across multiple skills such as worktree-start and worktree-end. Related to #479

Background: tests/fix-1133-oracle-mark-outline-detail.sh, added in PR #1213, is 680 lines. It exceeds the HARD limit (>500 lines) in rules/coding/file-split.md and requires immediate action. Related to #1175 (splitting a different file with the same pattern).

Extract common setup/teardown into a shared helper. Related to #1175


### FEATURE: fix: update-docs UD-8g/UD-9e emit premature WORKFLOW_USER_VERIFIED before PR exists (#1053 follow-up) (2026-06-28, #1219)
Background: PR #1056 (issue #1053, commit a770075) replaced the prose WF-CODE-N workflow with an oracle-driven state machine, introducing a dedicated `user_verification` oracle step. `skills/update-docs/SKILL.md` was left instructing emission of `<<WORKFLOW_USER_VERIFIED>>` in **UD-8g** (Path A, `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on`) and **UD-9e** (Path B). Under `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on` this is wrong: it contradicts the oracle's own `user_verification` hint (`bin/workflow/next-step` — "do NOT emit here; defer to /worktree-end WE-8") and `skills/_shared/user-verified.md` ("Never emit earlier in the workflow — the PR must exist first"). The #1053 refactor removed `and invoke commit-push` from UD-8g but failed to remove the premature emit directive itself. Observed: during the docs step on the linked-worktree side (before any PR exists), CC tries to emit `WORKFLOW_USER_VERIFIED`.
Changes: Delete UD-8g and UD-9e. `update-docs` ends at its `docs_complete` emit (UD-8f / UD-9d); the oracle owns the subsequent `user_verification` step and drives it per `ENFORCE_WORKTREE` mode (on -> defer to `/worktree-end`; off -> emit just before `/commit-push`). This restores single ownership of `user_verification` by the oracle. Related to #1122 (sibling in the same #1053 oracle-refactor defect class — that issue covers CC stopping at stage boundaries instead of auto-proceeding). Related to #1122


### FEATURE: triage-flag propagation: deferred follow-ups from #558 (reviewer parity, codex-loop SSOT, parser robustness) (2026-07-03, #578)
Background: When implementing MUST/OPTIONAL/NA propagation to the planner / codex in #558, the Codex adversarial review (`review-code-codex`) raised three concerns. These are out of scope for that session (already agreed in intent.md `## Accepted Tradeoffs` paragraph 2 that reviewer extension would be split into a separate issue; the other two are also independent design decisions), so this issue groups and tracks them. ### Concern 1 — reviewer parity (HIGH) `agents/outline-reviewer.md` / `agents/detail-reviewer.md` do not recognize the new enum (MUST / OPTIONAL / NA) and only check the legacy `disposition: fix in scope`. If Codex is unavailable or returns a malformed verdict, the reviewer-only fallback path is missing the coverage check for class members tagged `MUST`. **Source:** review-code-codex output (2026-05-26, post-#558 implementation) **Quote:** "When Codex is unavailable or malformed, plans with new `disposition: MUST` members can be approved without any class-member coverage check. That is a direct orthogonality gap in a symmetric review path." ### Concern 2 — codex-review-loop.md SSOT (HIGH) `skills/_shared/codex-review-loop.md` documents as SSOT the contract of "using codex's SKIPPED/FAILED stdout as the fallback trigger." The non-zero exit-code propagation for triage-split.sh fail-loud introduced in #558 was added to the individual SKILL.md files (`make-outline-plan` / `make-detail-plan`), but is not reflected in the shared loop spec. There is a risk that other callers (including future consumers) that follow the shared spec literally will swallow the non-zero exit. **Quote:** "Since `review-plan-codex` now exits non-zero on triage failure, any caller following the shared loop literally can still swallow or misroute the failure." ### Concern 3 — parser robustness (LOW) The parser in `skills/_shared/triage-split.sh` requires `<space> + <em-dash U+2014> + ' disposition: '` as a literal delimiter. If a human or an LLM writes it with an ASCII hyphen (`-`), an en-dash (`–`), or extra spaces, it fail-louds (exit 3). #558 made the design decision to "fail loud rather than allow silent corruption," but there is room to add punctuation normalization upstream. **Quote:** "existing markdown produced with ASCII hyphen, en dash, extra spaces, or normalized punctuation will hard-fail [...] this is a reliability risk unless the exact delimiter is already enforced everywhere upstream."
Changes: Each concern can be implemented as an independent PR, but all are subtasks toward the completed form of the #554 parent triage redesign. Proposed order: ### Task A — reviewer parity (Concern 1) - Update `agents/outline-reviewer.md` / `agents/detail-reviewer.md` to consume the pre-tiered MUST/OPTIONAL/NA list - Inject the `triage-split.sh` result at the point where the orchestrator skill (`make-outline-plan` / `make-detail-plan`) invokes the reviewer - Reviewer prompt assertion: reject plans with unaddressed MUST items, require a rationale for skipping OPTIONAL items ### Task B — codex-review-loop.md SSOT (Concern 2) - Document as spec in `skills/_shared/codex-review-loop.md` the mandatory handling (`rc=$?` pattern) for `review-plan-codex`'s non-zero exit - Add a prohibition clause against the `bash \` antipattern - Align with the existing individual SKILL.md implementations ### Task C — parser robustness (Concern 3) - Add tolerance for ASCII hyphen / en-dash / extra whitespace to the parser in `skills/_shared/triage-split.sh` - Or normalize at markdown-generation time upstream (`clarify-intent`) - The latter is preferable per the SSOT principle (parser stays strict, normalization happens at the generation side) ## Acceptance criteria - Task A / B / C each merge as an independent PR (or may be combined into a single PR) - Existing #558-derived tests (`tests/feature-558-*.sh`) continue to pass - E2E test the fail-loud / coverage check on the reviewer path ## Out of scope (reiterated) - No behavior change needed for `bin/extract-mandatory-sections` / `skills/_shared/assemble-mandatory.sh` - No intent.md schema change needed (already completed by this issue's predecessor: #558) ## Related - Parent: #554 (Group: clarify-intent Class members triage redesign) - Sibling (closed): #558 (MUST/OPTIONAL/NA propagation to planner / codex) - Source: `review-code-codex` output during the #558 implementation session 20260526-135551


### FEATURE: make-detail-plan / make-outline-plan: cap-menu does not show blocking concerns, so the rationale for the recommended choice is unclear (2026-07-03, #623)
Background: In the cap-menu-dispatch step of `make-detail-plan` and `make-outline-plan`, the AskUserQuestion only shows the "Land / Adjust / Extend" choices, with no display at all of the current blocking concerns (codex review findings) or the rationale for the Recommended choice. Under `CONFIRM_DETAIL=off` / `CONFIRM_OUTLINE=off` settings, the loop proceeds without intermediate confirmation, so the cap-menu is the only user confirmation point. If the concerns are not visible on that screen, the user can only mechanically pick Recommended and cannot actually make an informed decision.
Changes: Add the following to the AskUserQuestion at cap-menu-dispatch time: - The list of blocking concerns from the latest round (with severity) - The Recommended choice and its rationale (e.g. recommend Extend when concerns are HIGH only, etc.) Target files: - `skills/_shared/cap-menu-dispatch.md` — add a concerns-display step to the AskUserQuestion call spec - `bin/review-loop-cap-menu` — either add a concerns field to the menu JSON, or have the orchestrator read RAW_FILE and embed it into the question string Related to #603

### FEATURE: fix: parent-close-proposal-prepare.sh uses REST API for parent detection but GitHub only exposes sub-issue parent via GraphQL — G.5 cascade never fires (2026-07-03, #1285)
Background: `parent-close-proposal-prepare.sh` detects whether a closed sub-issue has a parent eligible for G.5 cascade (ICF-F recursion in `issue-close-finalize`). It calls `gh api "repos/$REPO/issues/$N" --jq '.parent.number // empty'`. The GitHub REST API (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/{N}`) does not include a `.parent` field in its response — parent-child sub-issue relationships are only accessible via GraphQL. As a result, `PARENT` is always empty, the script always exits 1, and the G.5 cascade never fires. Meta parent issues are never auto-closed when all their sub-issues complete. Verified: `gh api "repos/nirecom/agents/issues/1231" --jq '.parent // "NULL"'` → `NULL`. The bug affects every session where a sub-issue closes and the meta parent should cascade-close.
Changes: Replace the REST API call in `bin/github-issues/parent-close-proposal-prepare.sh` with a GraphQL query to fetch the parent issue number for a given sub-issue. Use the same `gh api graphql` pattern documented in `rules/github-issues.md` for sub-issue operations (e.g., `repository { issue(number: N) { parent { number } } }` or equivalent parent-node query). This restores G.5 cascade functionality so meta parent issues auto-close when all sub-issues complete.


### FEATURE: Add /workflow-off skill and pre-commit human-bypass (TTY detection) (2026-07-05, #1337)
Background: There are two problems. **1. Sentinels cannot be invoked from a skill:** `workflow-gate.js` blocks commits when `workflow_init` is not complete. It also blocks single-file edits in docs-only repos (e.g. my-specs-repo). `ENFORCE_WORKTREE_EXCLUDE` is specific to `enforce-worktree.js` and `workflow-gate.js` does not reference it. The sentinels `<<WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKFLOW_OFF: {reason}>>` / `<<WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKFLOW_ON: {reason}>>` are already implemented and work, but cannot be invoked from a skill (only via an inline `echo` during the conversation). **2. Human terminal git is restricted:** `hooks/pre-commit` is distinct from CC's PreToolUse and fires whenever `git commit` runs, regardless of whether a human or CC executed it. git commands typed by a human in the terminal get blocked by the `ENFORCE_WORKTREE` check. CC's git is already blocked at PreToolUse (`enforce-worktree.js`); pre-commit is the second layer of defense-in-depth. Commits from a human's terminal should bypass it.
Changes: 1. Create `skills/workflow-off/SKILL.md` — emits the sentinel `<<WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKFLOW_OFF: {reason}>>`. 2. Create `skills/workflow-on/SKILL.md` — symmetrically emits the sentinel `<<WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKFLOW_ON: {reason}>>`. 3. Add a human bypass to the `ENFORCE_WORKTREE` section of `hooks/pre-commit` — a combination of TTY detection (`[ -t 1 ]`) and the `CLAUDECODE` environment variable. 4. Add `user-invocable: false` to the frontmatter of `skills/make-outline-plan/SKILL.md` and `skills/make-detail-plan/SKILL.md`.

### FEATURE: PR #1420 — feature/canary5-6git (2026-07-13, 79547ed278cb7d8836b8db76c85a602af628fa12, #1420)

Background: feat(#1400,#1401): WRITE_PATTERNS→IR migration — retire green-group + git, typed write-target contract, fail-closed security convergence

Changes: FEATURE #1400: canary-5 — retire 12 "green-group" WRITE_PATTERNS surface-regex entries (posix-redir ×2, pwsh ×7, file-op rm/cp/mv ×3) from `hooks/lib/bash-write-patterns/patterns.js` and `STRIP_KINDS`. Write-detection for these groups is now performed exclusively by the IR-based predicates `isPosixRedirWriteIR`, `isPwshWriteIR`, and `isFileOpWriteIR` in `bash-write-targets.js`. Added typed write-target contract `{resolveVia:"ancestor"|"self", path}` at the `collectWriteTargetsFromSegments` collector layer; all downstream consumers (`bash-write-scope.js`, block hooks) updated to use `.path`. Migrate sibling test suites (feature-parallel-sessions-worktree-bash-patterns, fix-1391, fix-416, refactor-1294, fix-876, fix-fd-redirect) to assert new `classify()=="read"` + IR predicate contract.;FEATURE #1401: canary-6-git — retire 18 git WRITE_PATTERNS surface-regex entries. Introduce `isGitWriteIR` (read-allowlist + unknown→write fail-closed, basename-aware for /usr/bin/git/git.exe) in new `hooks/lib/bash-write-patterns/git-write-ir.js`. Add `extractGitWriteTargets` in `hooks/lib/bash-write-targets/git.js` returning `{resolveVia:"self", path:repoRoot}` self-target. Redesign `findRepoRootForBash` to resolve scope per write-segment's own IR argv (segment-aware, quote-aware; cross-segment/quoted --work-tree mis-scope → fail-closed to in-session). Add `resolveGitSubArgv` (skips git global flags via parse-git-args.js SSOT). Add `peelWrappers`/`scanWrappedVerb` with basename lookup so `/usr/bin/env git commit`, `/usr/bin/nice git commit` are caught. Add `isEverySegmentExcluded` git/gh per-segment write predicate (prevents `cp … && git commit` allow bypass). Add `isCommandSubstWriteIR` (writes in `$(…)`/backtick), `isNewlineInjectedWriteIR` (newline-injected writes), `isExoticExecWriteIR` (eval/xargs/find-exec/process-subst/sh-c/pwsh-Command). All exotic predicates wired into fast-allow gate, `isEverySegmentExcluded`, and `isReadOnlyInterpreterC`. Security: all fail-closed (unknown subcommand→write, ambiguous scope→in-session block, dynamic eval body→write). Canary suite 326 cases green. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/canary5-6git pr=#1420 -->

Codex security review found and this session fixed a HIGH own-worktree false-ambiguity bug (subdirectory CWD collected own root as a sibling) via fail-before-fix tests. ### FEATURE: PR #1459 — fix/enforce-worktree-ir-regression (2026-07-17, ff050931, #1459)

Background: fix(enforce-worktree): restore scratchpad-redirect and New-Item-Directory allow paths lost in PR #1420 IR-signature migration

Changes: #1109: Investigated the 8 reported main-worktree false-blocks attributed to PR #1420's `collectBashWriteTargets` IR-signature migration (`(cmd)` → `(ir, repoRoot)`). The plans-dir heredoc / `node -e` writes were already green (PR #1179); the residual scratchpad-redirect variants were the regression. Fixed by teaching `expandStaticShellTokens` to expand `$SCRATCHPAD`/`${SCRATCHPAD}` only when they resolve under `<os-tmpdir>/claude/`, and adding the `areAllBashTargetsUnderClaude` allow predicate (fail-closed: allowed only when ALL targets resolve under the session scratchpad, tightened to `$SCRATCHPAD` when set). New SSOT `hooks/lib/claude-scratchpad-base.js` guards against TEMP/TMP poisoning by excluding paths that resolve inside the repo.;#1441: Restored the `New-Item -ItemType Directory` allow path that PR #1420 dropped when pwsh WRITE_PATTERNS were retired (on Windows, `findRepoRoot` on a non-existent external path fell back to process CWD → main repo root → block). Extracted the logic into `hooks/enforce-worktree/main-worktree-allows/new-item.js` with head-anchored dispatch, argv0 verification, and PowerShell single-quote literal semantics (`'$SCRATCHPAD/x'` stays literal → in-repo → block). The supervisor scratchpad stdout-redirect gap is covered by the same `areAllBashTargetsUnderClaude` predicate.;#1290: Same New-Item root cause as #1441 — `New-Item -ItemType Directory` issued during `/worktree-start` WS-5 (sanctioned parent-dir creation) is allowed again through the restored branch. Kept fail-closed for `-ItemType File`, in-repo targets, and chained commands.;#923: Confirmed `git worktree remove` (and add/prune) from the main worktree is allowed after restructuring `isAllowedWorktreeCommand` into a head-first dispatch (git-worktree vs New-Item) and splitting the 600-line module to satisfy the 500-line HARD limit (`worktree-command.js`, `worker-script.js`, `new-item.js`). Added a permanent regression canary (`tests/fix-1441-new-item-scratchpad-allow.sh`) so the restored allows cannot silently regress again. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/enforce-worktree-ir-regression pr=#1459 -->

Wired actionableOnly:true in stop-l2-findings-display.js (Stop hook hard guarantee). Constrained supervisor.md Reporting back section to fixed one-line ack. Added bin/supervisor-render-alert CLI for manual debugging. Security: applied escapeTokens+newline-collapse in actionableOnly branch (sibling parity with forFinalReport); added SESSION_ID_RE validation to CLI.

### FEATURE: PR #1490 — feature/1423-write-detector-test (2026-07-17, 64ce86f7b001fa4617dabdfd464ae4d57e67155d, #1490)
Background: feat(enforce-worktree): report write predicate name in block reason
Changes: Background: `enforce-worktree.js` block responses were opaque — when a Bash write command was blocked, the reason message did not identify which write-detection predicate fired, making it difficult to understand and debug unexpected blocks.;Changes: Added `hooks/enforce-worktree/write-detector.js` (`detectWritePredicate(ir)`) — checks all 13 write predicates in order and returns the first match as `{name, detail}`; wired `_writeDetector` variable in the `Bash` branch of `enforce-worktree.js`; appended `\nDetected by: <detail> (<name>)` suffix to all 5 block-site reasons (3 generic + 2 gh-specific). Added L2 test `tests/feature-1423-enforce-worktree-reject-reason.sh` covering all 13 predicate families (14 assertions, all green). <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/1423-write-detector-test pr=#1490 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1489 — fix/fix-1483-issue-create-allowlist (2026-07-17, 90c5537, #1489)
Background: fix(#1483): add missing scripts to enforce-worktree SANCTIONED allowlist
Changes: fix(#1483): Add missing scripts to enforce-worktree SANCTIONED allowlist — `bin/github-issues/issue-create-dispatch.sh`, `skills/issue-create/scripts/run-bulk-dispatch.sh`, and `skills/issue-create/scripts/run-phase5-record.sh` were absent from the SANCTIONED array in `hooks/enforce-worktree/main-worktree-allows/worker-script.js`. The `ISSUE_CREATE_SKILL=1` prefix only bypasses `enforce-issue-close.js`, not `enforce-worktree.js`, so calling these scripts from the main worktree was blocked. The false claim in `skills/issue-create/SKILL.md` ("callable from main worktree") was replaced with accurate guidance requiring a linked worktree when `ENFORCE_WORKTREE=on`. Repeated blocks accumulated supervisor warnings and sealed the WORKTREE_OFF escape hatch, causing a two-level deadlock that required manual intervention. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/fix-1483-issue-create-allowlist pr=#1489 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1491 — fix/1145-cleanup-window (2026-07-17, 9d1c1e5fab84e46c5b5a5186487cccbea4c248e7, #1491)
Background: fix(#1145): narrow isWorktreeEndEnv() to WE-15..WE-22 cleanup window
Changes: BUGFIX #1145: isWorktreeEndEnv() post-WE-22 false-positive fixed — new worktree-cleanup-marker.js CLI creates <sid>-wt-cleanup-active at WE-14b and deletes at WE-22a; isWorktreeEndEnv() now checks marker existence (not final-report-env.json content); anchor and shim tests updated with marker-file fixtures; T-anchor-6, T4j (KEY REGRESSION) and feature-1145-cleanup-marker.sh added. Test gap: no test for post-WE-22 scenario (env-json present + marker absent → false). <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/1145-cleanup-window pr=#1491 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1494 — fix/write-detector-1424-1425-1448 (2026-07-17, 36d0102, #1494)
Background: fix(enforce-worktree): write-detector false-negatives/false-positives (#1424 #1425 #1448)
Changes: fix(#1424): isGhWriteIR first-segment-only false-negative — `isGhWriteIR` looped over IR segments with `break` after finding the first `gh` segment, causing it to evaluate only that segment. A sequenced command like `gh status && gh pr merge 123` returned false (first segment is read-only). Fixed by removing `break` and evaluating each segment immediately; mirrors `isGitWriteIR` per-segment pattern (CPR-5 symmetry).;fix(#1425): isNewlineInjectedWriteIR backslash-continuation false-positive — `isNewlineInjectedWriteIR` split lines on `[\r\n]+` before folding `\`+newline continuations, causing continued lines to be passed as independent commands to `innerCommandIsWrite`. `isInterpreterCWriteIR` then matched `bash -c` inside quoted argument values on those stripped continuation lines, triggering false-positive blocks on sanctioned multi-line supervisor-report calls. Fixed by inserting `stripped.replace(/\\[ \t]*\r?\n/g, ' ')` (fold) before split, preserving quoting context.;fix(#1448): write-detector false-positives on non-repo redirect and trailing read-only suffix — (A) Sequenced commands with a trailing read-only segment (e.g. `git log > /tmp/out; echo RC=$?`) fell through to `abstain` because `hasCommandSequencingOutsideHeredoc` returned early before any target scope check. Fixed by adding `areAllWriteSegmentsOutsideSessionScope` (new function in `bash-write-scope.js`) that classifies each segment as read / target-write / targetless-write and allows when all write-segments' targets are outside session scope; targetless-write (gh, interpreter, exotic) remains fail-closed. (B) From a non-git CWD, single-command redirects to paths outside any git repo (e.g. `/tmp`) were blocked because `repoRoot` was null and the Bug 2 allow path required `repoRoot` to be truthy. Fixed by adding `targets.every(t => findRepoRoot(t.path) === null)` as an additional disjunct, allowing writes to provably non-git paths while preserving the existing block for targets that resolve inside any git repo. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/write-detector-1424-1425-1448 pr=#1494 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1493 — feature/1482-post-compact-progress (2026-07-17, 2100697093f8898e77e474a79a6185e22bc9ef20, #1493)
Background: fix(#1482,#861,#1161): post-compact workflow progress + conv-lang any no-op + pr-merge reset_reason
Changes: BUGFIX #861: conv-lang.js — `CONV_LANG=any` was not in the no-op list, causing spurious "Respond to the user in any." injection. Added `any` (case-insensitive after normalize) alongside `english` and empty-string. Test gap: no test for `CONV_LANG=any` return value in existing unit-helper.sh.;BUGFIX #1161: workflow-mark.js — `gh pr merge` detection reset `user_verification` to pending without recording why. Added `{ reset_reason: "post-merge" }` as extraFields to `markStep()`, so post-compact can annotate the step as "(reset after pr merge — expected)" rather than leaving the reader to wonder whether the reset was intentional. Test gap: no integration test for the `reset_reason` field on `user_verification` after pr-merge detection.;FEATURE #1482: post-compact.js — resumed assistants had only the lossy LLM-generated compaction summary to infer workflow progress. Added an inline 10-step workflow progress block (WORKFLOW_STEPS constant, direct `readState()` call) to the PostCompact `additionalContext` injection. Also surfaces `cwd`/`git_branch` from state and annotates `user_verification: pending` with "(reset after pr merge — expected)" when `reset_reason === "post-merge"` (pairs with #1161). Fail-open: missing/corrupt state file emits "(no state file found — run /workflow-init)". <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/1482-post-compact-progress pr=#1493 -->

### FEATURE: PR #1496 — feature/issue-1492-label-taxonomy (2026-07-17, 8600877, #1496)
Background: feat(#1492): rename model:* labels to reporter-model:*, add model-scope:* taxonomy
Changes: Renamed `model:*` label series to `reporter-model:*` (fable/opus/sonnet/ds4) to distinguish "which model reported this issue" (reliability signal) from "which model exhibits this behavior". Added `model-scope:*` series (ds4/devstral/qwen-coder/claude) as an optional label for issues scoped to a specific model's behavior. Added `reporter-model:devstral` and `reporter-model:qwen-coder` for local LLMs running via llama-swap. Created `bin/github-issues/migrate-model-labels.sh` to automate the GitHub-native label rename (RENAME → CREATE → model:others cleanup → #1488 title fix), with dry-run mode and input validation matching the sibling sync-labels.sh. Background: `model:*` conflated two concepts; `[ds4]` title prefix workaround was unergonomic. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/issue-1492-label-taxonomy pr=#1496 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1497 — feature/issue-1487-gh-scope-warn (2026-07-17, d298e401901a6cc2d731abc4ebebf5d2b380a6b6, #1497)
Background: feat(#1487): add soft gh auth 'project' scope check to install.sh + install.ps1
Changes: FEATURE feat(#1487): add soft gh auth 'project' scope check to install.sh + install.ps1 | Background: New users who had not run 'gh auth refresh -s project' got an opaque GraphQL error when using /issue-create or Projects v2; no onboarding guidance existed in install.sh or install.ps1. | Changes: install.sh and install.ps1 each gained a 3-stage soft check (gh present → auth status exit-0 → scope grep); if 'project' scope is absent, a yellow notice on stderr suggests 'gh auth refresh -s project' or '/issue-setup'. Non-blocking (install exits 0 regardless). New L2 test: tests/feature-1487-install-gh-scope-warn.sh (4 cases covering absent-gh / not-authed / scope-present / scope-absent; install.ps1 PowerShell path tracked as L3 gap in issue #1487). <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/issue-1487-gh-scope-warn pr=#1497 -->

Background: fix(#1485): add bash/node prefix to direct call sites + chmod+x sweep on bin/

Background: fix(#1499): add ISSUE_TOKEN_CLI_GUARD_RE to parseIssueToken — reject bare digits
Changes: BUGFIX #1499: `parseIssueToken` in `hooks/lib/parse-closes-issues.js` accepted bare digit strings (e.g. `"3.2"`) as issue numbers because `ISSUE_TOKEN_BODY_RE` made the `#` prefix optional. ### FEATURE: PR #1507 — feature/1492-label-sync-delete (2026-07-17, 1e99674551dc0d81bc9cda791bc700d5cd6515c2, #1507)
Background: feat(#1492): sync-labels DELETE propagation + migrate pre-delete guard
Changes: feat(#1492): sync-labels.sh DELETE propagation + migrate pre-delete guard — Background: Two related fixes: (1) migrate-model-labels.sh Phase 1 was blocked when CI pre-created empty reporter-model:* labels before the rename script ran, losing issue associations for all 46 model:* issues; (2) sync-labels.sh and propagate-labels.sh had no mechanism to delete labels removed from labels.yml, leaving orphaned labels across sibling repos. Changes: Added pre-delete guard to migrate-model-labels.sh Phase 1 loop (checks for zero-issue target label, deletes it, then proceeds with native gh label edit --name rename). ### BUGFIX: PR #1512 — feature/1488-malformed-tokens (2026-07-18, 19cd50b82b90df5eb85bcb9c8c19500f6ca021b6, #1512)
Background: fix(#1488): validate workflow-init-driver raw-token args — reject newline/malformed tokens with NEXT_HINT
Changes: Added `validateTokens()` to `bin/workflow/workflow-init-driver` with 3-stage guard: LF/CR → `invalid_token_newline`; no `#\d` prefix or trailing non-digit junk (`!/^[^#]*#\d+$/`) → `invalid_token_format`; length > 64 → `invalid_token_format`. Guard fires in `main()` before `resolveSessionId()` and emits `ACTION=blocked REASON=... NEXT_HINT=...` to stdout (stderr is suppressed by caller via `2>/dev/null`). Catches ds4 multi-line prompt injection at the entry point before `isNewlineInjectedWriteIR` can false-positive on ENFORCE_WORKTREE. `ISSUE_TOKEN_CLI_GUARD_RE` required from `hooks/lib/parse-closes-issues.js` (CPR-2 — not copied). Extended `emitBlocked()` in `directive.js` with optional `NEXT_HINT=` third parameter. Added `<raw-tokens>` explanation line in `SKILL.md` WI-2. New test file `tests/feature-workflow-init-driver/driver-malformed-tokens.sh` (49 assertions, 9 cases M1-M9). <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/1488-malformed-tokens pr=#1512 -->
Test gap: no L3 test for real claude -p + non-native LLM session driving workflow-init; L2 driver-malformed-tokens.sh covers validateTokens() guard fully

### FEATURE: PR #1518 — fix/fix-enforce-worktree-false-blocks (2026-07-18, 314e449, #1518)
Background: fix(enforce-worktree): remove wtCount upper-bound for skill-prefixed stash (#1024)
Changes: issue:#1115 category:BUGFIX subject:"fix(enforce-worktree): POSIX fd-dup redirects (2>&1, N>&1, N>&-) false-block sanctioned git commands via hasShellChaining" background:"hasShellChaining() and rejectInterpreterAndChaining() in shared-cmd-utils.js used the character class [|;&] which matches a lone & character. POSIX fd-dup redirects (2>&1, >&1, N>&-) contain an unquoted & that is not a chaining operator, causing sanctioned git commands with trailing redirects (e.g. git worktree add <path> <branch> 2>&1) to be false-blocked." changes:"Added /\d*>&\d+|\d*>&-/g fd-dup stripping in hasShellChaining() and rejectInterpreterAndChaining() before the chaining character test. &> and &>> redirect-both forms are intentionally not stripped so write targets remain visible to collectBashWriteTargets. New test cases in tests/fix-enforce-worktree-merge-gate.sh and tests/fix-595-bypass-e2e.sh.";issue:#982 category:BUGFIX subject:"fix(enforce-worktree): isAllowedWorktreeCommand unconditional-allow path blocked by hasShellChaining fd-dup false positive (recurrence of #881)" background:"The unconditional-allow path in isAllowedWorktreeCommand for git worktree remove/prune/add was guarded by hasShellChaining(). The [|;&] character class in hasShellChaining() matched lone & in POSIX fd-dup redirects, blocking sanctioned worktree lifecycle commands when the command string contained a redirect like 2>&1." changes:"Covered by the same hasShellChaining/rejectInterpreterAndChaining fd-dup stripping fix as #1115 (shared-cmd-utils.js). No additional files changed specifically for #982.";issue:#923 category:BUGFIX subject:"fix(enforce-worktree): git -C <path> worktree remove/prune false-blocked from main-wt CWD — isAllowedWorktreeCommand did not recognize the -C flag form" background:"isAllowedWorktreeCommand() matched git worktree subcommands by position assuming git was the first token. When git -C <path> worktree remove was issued from the main-worktree CWD, the -C flag shifted the subcommand position and the regex did not match, causing the predicate to return false and block the command." changes:"Added -C path validation to isAllowedWorktreeCommand() in main-worktree-allows/standard.js: rejects multiple -C flags; when -C is present, the argument must resolve case-insensitively to repoRoot. New test cases T923-3 through T923-7 in tests/fix-923-enforce-worktree-c-flag-main-worktree.sh and E-WT-CFLAG cases in tests/fix-595-bypass-e2e.sh.";issue:#838 category:BUGFIX subject:"fix(enforce-worktree): git -C <main-worktree> worktree remove/prune blocked from linked-wt CWD — -C form not recognized by isAllowedWorktreeCommand" background:"When a session ran git -C <main-worktree-path> worktree remove from a linked worktree CWD, isAllowedWorktreeCommand did not validate the -C argument and failed-closed on -C forms it did not recognize, blocking the sanctioned worktree lifecycle command." changes:"Covered by the same isAllowedWorktreeCommand -C validation fix as #923 (main-worktree-allows/standard.js). The -C path is resolved and compared to repoRoot case-insensitively. Tests in tests/fix-923-enforce-worktree-c-flag-main-worktree.sh.";issue:#959 category:BUGFIX subject:"fix(enforce-worktree): sanctioned worker scripts with linked-wt path arguments false-blocked from main-wt CWD — add isAllowedWorkerScriptInvocation predicate" background:"Sanctioned worker scripts (issue-close-stage-worker, commit-push-worker etc.) are launched via bash \"<AGENTS_CONFIG_DIR>/bin/<script>\" with linked-worktree absolute paths as log-redirect targets. When called from the main-worktree CWD, the enforce-worktree main-worktree write guard treated the linked-wt path arguments as main-wt write targets and blocked the invocation." changes:"Added isAllowedWorkerScriptInvocation(cmd, repoRoot) predicate to hooks/enforce-worktree/main-worktree-allows/standard.js. Identity gate: cmd must match bash \"<double-quoted-sanctioned-script>\" against a 5-entry SANCTIONED list. argTail gate: rejects chaining operators and bare & background operator (using /&(?!>)/ to distinguish background from redirect forms). Write target gate: all targets must resolve inside registered linked worktrees (git -C repoRoot worktree list --porcelain); fail-closed on any parse failure or out-of-registry path. Wired into enforce-worktree.js main-worktree allow chain. New tests in tests/fix-959-enforce-worktree-worker-path-arg.sh (8 cases including a security pin for bare & backgrounding).";issue:#1024 category:BUGFIX subject:"fix(enforce-worktree): isAllowedMainWorktreeCleanup false-blocks WORKTREE_END_SKILL=1 git stash pop when zombie worktrees inflate wtCount beyond cap" background:"isAllowedMainWorktreeCleanup in standard.js used a wtCount upper-bound check (wtCount <= maxCount, maxCount = skillPrefixed ? 2 : 1). In repos accumulating zombie worktrees (e.g. 54 registered entries), wtCount > maxCount caused WORKTREE_END_SKILL=1 git -C <main> stash pop to be blocked during /worktree-end WE-20 with 'write blocked. Reason: main worktree'. PR #1126 (2026-06-26) had already added pop/drop/clear to ALLOWED_STASH but did not address the wtCount cap." changes:"Removed the wtCount upper-bound for skill-prefixed stash subcommands only: when skillPrefixed=true and sub==='stash', the function returns true after the wtCount>=1 check, bypassing the maxCount cap. restore and checkout retain the existing cap (wtCount<=2 for skill-prefixed). wtCount<1 early-return preserves fail-closed behavior. JSDoc updated to reflect the new per-subcommand upper-bound policy. New tests S34b (wtCount=4 zombie scenario stash → allow), S34c (no-prefix stash unchanged → block), S34d (skill-prefixed restore wtCount=3 → block), S34e (skill-prefixed checkout wtCount=3 → block). S34 expectation changed from assert_block to assert_allow." <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/fix-enforce-worktree-false-blocks pr=#1518 -->

### FEATURE: PR #1525 — fix/scan-outbound-fixes (2026-07-19, 9c789cb4ba8b382183d20f68d9d586d581760890, #1525)
Background: fix: scan-outbound trailing-newline guard, gh api write scanning, test stub regression check
Changes: fix(#1520): `bin/scan-outbound.sh` loadAllowlist/loadBlocklist loops (`while IFS= read -r line`) dropped the last line of allowlist/blocklist files that lacked a trailing newline; added `|| [ -n "$line" ]` guard after each loop (lines 41 and 54) to flush the final partial read — matching the existing correct form at line 307.;fix(#714): `hooks/lib/forge-write-extract.js` `isForgeScanTarget()` did not recognize `gh api -X METHOD` / `gh api --method METHOD` write commands (POST/PATCH/PUT/DELETE) as forge-scan targets; added `GH_API_WRITE_REGEX`, `extractApiFieldTexts()` (handles `-f`/`-F`/`--field`/`--input @file`), and updated `extractTexts()` dispatch to cover `gh api` write payloads.;fix(#1035): `tests/feature-990-scan-outbound-offensive-integration.sh` stub at `tests/feature-990-scan-outbound-offensive-integration/scan-offensive` was a bash script incompatible with the hook's `spawnSync('node', ...)` invocation; added a regression assertion (T0) at the top of the test to verify the stub carries `#!/usr/bin/env node` shebang so this class of breakage is caught early. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/scan-outbound-fixes pr=#1525 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1529 — feature/outline-friction (2026-07-19, 5b8cf21e6d2496edb28687829546ee95ed6b9e49, #1529)
Background: feat(#1384,#1522,#478,#1124,#1146,#1245): reduce outline planning friction — frontrunner-collapse, abolish MOP-7 dialog, PLAN_LANG directives, VS Code text visibility
Changes: #1384: outline-planner gains frontrunner-collapse rule — when intent leaves implementation choice open but one approach clearly dominates on trade-offs, risk, cost, and existing-code consistency, outline-planner emits SINGLE_APPROACH_JUSTIFIED with frontrunner-collapse reason, suppressing the approach menu without requiring an intent-lock;#1522: MOP-7 AskUserQuestion abolished from make-outline-plan — orchestrator now auto-selects the recommended approach and emits a turn-final prose rationale; confirmation reduced from two interactions (MOP-7 dialog + MOP-8 sentinel) to one (MOP-8 sentinel only) on CONFIRM_OUTLINE=on; "Pass all approaches" bypass option removed;#478: outline stage Accepted Tradeoffs constraint documented in make-outline-plan SKILL.md — outline-planner cannot add new ## Accepted Tradeoffs entries because assemble-mandatory.sh carries intent.md tradeoffs verbatim; planners directed to use ## Confirmed non-goals or ## Constraints for new design decisions;#1124: clarify-intent Remove-and-continue branch now syncs plan artifacts — when an issue is removed from closes_issues, the corresponding - #N: title line is removed from ## Issues in intent.md (and outline.md if present), preventing stale entries in downstream steps;#1146: make-outline-plan and make-detail-plan gain explicit PLAN_LANG draft directives — orchestrator-authored outline.md and detail.md bodies must be written in the configured PLAN_LANG from the first draft, eliminating the reactive re-write cycle triggered by check-plan-lang.js;#1245: user-facing analysis summaries in clarify-intent, make-outline-plan, and make-detail-plan must be delivered as turn-final assistant text or AskUserQuestion preview/description — mid-turn text between tool calls is invisible in the VS Code extension <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/outline-friction pr=#1529 -->

### FEATURE: PR #1535 — fix/fix-1528-shim-closed-phase (2026-07-19, d389fa959af8e605951bbeb733ec881b85c98361, #1535)
Background: fix(#1528): add TERMINAL_ALERT_PHASES constant; supervisor shim/guard closed+paused bypass
Changes: issue:#1528 category:BUGFIX subject:"fix(#1528): supervisor-off-proposal-shim alert_phase=closed/paused not bypassing OFF-sentinel block" background:"The shim's early-return condition only checked alertPhase === \"done\", leaving \"closed\" (set by session-close SC-6) and \"paused\" (retry-exhaustion freeze) uncovered. When layer1.findings contained warning-or-higher entries after session-close, WORKFLOW_ENFORCE_WORKTREE_OFF was incorrectly blocked. #1426 added the \"done\" case but left \"closed\" and \"paused\" unhandled. Also fixed a pre-existing S9 test assertion stale since PR #1360 changed AUDIT_SEVERITY_THRESHOLD from \"error\" to \"warning\" without updating the test." changes:"Added TERMINAL_ALERT_PHASES = new Set([\"done\",\"paused\",\"closed\"]) to hooks/lib/supervisor-state-schema.js and exported it alongside ALERT_PHASE_VALUES. Replaced alertPhase === \"done\" in supervisor-off-proposal-shim.js with TERMINAL_ALERT_PHASES.has(alertPhase). Replaced 4 inline alertPhase !== \"done\" && alertPhase !== \"paused\" && alertPhase !== \"closed\" chains in supervisor-guard.js with !TERMINAL_ALERT_PHASES.has(alertPhase) (CPR-5 Orthogonality refactor). Fixed S9 assertion in feature-228-supervisor-state-schema.sh: \"error\" → \"warning\"." test_gap:"no test for alert_phase=closed and alert_phase=paused bypass in feature-supervisor-shim-injection.sh" <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/fix-1528-shim-closed-phase pr=#1535 -->

### FEATURE: PR #1539 — fix/1492-propagate-labels-path-format (2026-07-19, d17515bfce9593510c082d5598f10c255a510889, #1539)
Background: fix(#1492): PROPAGATE_LABELS_REPOS format — semicolon-separated absolute paths
Changes: category=BUGFIX subject="PROPAGATE_LABELS_REPOS format: semicolon-separated absolute paths" background="#1492 was reopened after PR #1507 (DELETE propagation) for a CPR-5 Orthogonality violation: PROPAGATE_LABELS_REPOS used space-separated owner/repo strings while ENFORCE_WORKTREE_ADDITIONAL_REPOS used semicolon-separated absolute paths. Both variables hold repository lists; different formats violated CPR-5." changes="Changed PROPAGATE_LABELS_REPOS to semicolon-separated absolute directory paths. propagate-labels.sh now resolves owner/repo at runtime via git -C <path> remote get-url origin, supporting both HTTPS and SSH GitHub remote URL formats. Background: fix(#1542): add CI fallback in propagate-labels.sh; pass PROPAGATE_LABELS_REPOS in sync-labels.yml
Changes: fix(#1542): add 2-stage resolver in propagate-labels.sh — stage 1 uses `[ -d ]` guard to run existing `git -C path remote get-url`; stage 2 falls back to `basename + AGENTS_WORKSPACE owner` for absolute paths absent in CI (ubuntu-latest). sync-labels.yml propagate job now receives `PROPAGATE_LABELS_REPOS` via `vars.PROPAGATE_LABELS_REPOS`. ### FEATURE: PR #1547 — fix/fix-923 (2026-07-19, 6cc55b4, #1547)
Background: fix(#923): normalize POSIX paths in isMainCheckout to fix worktree-remove block
Changes: category: BUGFIX subject: "fix(#923): isMainCheckout POSIX path normalization — git worktree remove blocked from main worktree" background: "`isMainCheckout(repoCwd)` in `hooks/enforce-worktree/git-repo-detection.js` passed `repoCwd` to `spawnSync` without normalizing POSIX-form paths (e.g., `/path/to/repo` from Git Bash on Windows). spawnSync returned ENOENT → `isMainCheckout` returned `false` → early-exit block's else branch fired → sanctioned `git worktree remove` blocked from main worktree. Third recurrence: PR #1157 (`5c58bb3`) and PR #1515 (`89ca051`) fixed symptoms at call-site / early-exit level without normalizing inside `isMainCheckout` itself, leaving the POSIX-path input class unhandled across all callers." changes: "Added `toWindowsPath` normalization guard at the start of `isMainCheckout` (symmetric with `findRepoRootForBash` lines 201-203; CPR-5 Orthogonality). `toWindowsPath` is a no-op on POSIX/macOS (CPR-8 Universality). All 4 callers of `isMainCheckout` in `enforce-worktree.js` now automatically protected. Added regression test `tests/fix-fix-923-posix-cwd.sh` that injects POSIX-form `toolInput.cwd` via `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1`, confirming the bug exists before the fix (T923-POSIX.A/B FAIL) and the security boundary is preserved (T923-POSIX.C PASS)." test_gap: "no test for POSIX-form `toolInput.cwd` in `fix-923-enforce-worktree-early-exit.sh` — the gap that allowed prior fixes to appear green while the Windows-specific regression persisted" <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/fix-923 pr=#1547 -->

Extended normalizeCwd to normalize C:/path to C:\path on Windows. Added backfillWriteTests to review-tests-handler.js COMPLETE path: auto-marks write_tests complete when staged test evidence exists, preventing ordering mismatch where review_tests completes before write_tests. Documented sentinel usage and linked-worktree CWD requirement in skills/write-tests/SKILL.md. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/1521-review-tests-backfill pr=#1551 -->

### FEATURE: PR #1556 — feature/issue-1544 (2026-07-20, 1552c05, #1556)
Background: feat(#1544): /issue-create reopen verdict — idempotent 3-point update
Changes: #1544: `/issue-create` reopen verdict now delegates to `bin/github-issues/reopen-with-update.sh`, which executes a 3-point idempotent sequence: (1) refresh a `<!-- BEGIN reopen-banner -->` block in the issue body with reopen count, UTC timestamp, and an 8-char SHA-256 of the session ID; (2) maintain a single `<!-- reopen-log -->` marker comment via REST PATCH (never stacking new comments); (3) apply `status:regressed` label (non-fatal on failure). Prevents a chronic misread where a reopen issue appeared "done" because only its body/first comments were reviewed. Session IDs are hashed before writing to the public issue body (CWE-200 prevention). Added `status:regressed` label to `.github/labels.yml`. Added `rules/github-issues.md` label table entry for `status:regressed`. Tests: DV2/DV2-b/DV2-c added to `tests/feature-issue-create-skill/section-dispatch-core.sh`; mock arms added to `section-mock.sh`. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/issue-1544 pr=#1556 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1550 — feature/943-e2e-per-hook-seam-l3 (2026-07-20, 79adf7acd3dcac7055f4652363c00862c3333218, #1550)
Background: feat(#943): add per-hook seam L3 tests for 6 workflow hooks; split test taxonomy to L1-L4
Changes: FEATURE #943: Add per-hook seam TL3 tests for 6 workflow hooks (workflow-mark, session-start, stop-confirm-plan-guard, stop-final-report-guard, subagent-start, post-compact) under permanent `tests/TL3-hook-<name>` names (17 files). Each test spawns a real `claude -p` mini-session to fire one hook and asserts side-effect files; subagent-start and post-compact land as TL3 gap entries (real event unfireable in short sessions). Fixed predictable temp-dir name (mkdtempSync) in 4 helpers.sh files.;REFACTOR (rules/test.md): split test-layer taxonomy to TL1–TL4, separating substrate-fidelity from pipeline-scope, and prefixed the layer tokens `TL` (Test Layer) so they no longer grep-collide with the local `L1:`/`L2:`/`L3:` case labels used inside individual test scripts. TL3 = single-component/seam real-environment (where #943 lands); TL4 = whole-pipeline end-to-end (the only tier named "E2E", tracked by #1543). Added filename convention: TL3/TL4 tests carry a layer prefix and must not embed an issue number (retire policies key on `feature-<N>` names and would delete permanent coverage). Renamed #943 tests to `TL3-hook-*`; propagated the TL tokens through `skills/_shared/test-design.md`, `rules/test/claude-e2e.md`, and `docs/architecture/claude-code/e2e-testing.md`. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/943-e2e-per-hook-seam-l3 pr=#1550 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1559 — fix/sync-labels-no-delete (2026-07-20, 4cebe820b2db8884cce152e6c06cd53e0b8c9a8c, #1559)
Background: feat(#1552): add --no-delete flag and protected: labels key to sync-labels.sh
Changes: Added `--no-delete` flag to `bin/github-issues/sync-labels.sh`: runs CREATE and UPDATE only, skipping every DELETE (logged `[NO-DELETE] Skipped delete`), mirroring the existing `--dry-run` guard in the DELETE dispatch case. Added a `protected:` top-level key to `.github/labels.yml` (parsed by a parameter-expansion-only bash loop into a CSV, passed to the awk diff engine via `-v protected_csv`) that permanently excludes the nine GitHub default labels (bug, enhancement, documentation, good first issue, invalid, duplicate, question, wontfix, help wanted) from DELETE even in a normal sync — sibling repos may still carry those labels on existing issues. `propagate-labels.sh` threads `--no-delete` to each sibling when `PROPAGATE_LABELS_NO_DELETE` is set (non-empty). `--no-delete` (temporary, full DELETE suppression at the bash dispatch layer) and `protected:` (permanent, per-label exclusion at the awk layer) are orthogonal mechanisms. Auth stays in the `gh` auth store (`gh auth token` fallback) — no token added to `.env.example`. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/sync-labels-no-delete pr=#1559 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1562 — fix/1557-audit-tests-retire (2026-07-20, b01aa976c05aa620ee4c442c8a35feaf580878a0, #1562)
Background: fix(#1557): audit-tests staleness via closed_at; add common orphan detector and scope tag enforcement
Changes: #1557: `bin/audit-tests.sh` staleness detection was broken since PR #689 (2026-06-01) reset all feature-NNN test committer dates via a frontmatter backfill, causing the tool to always return zero candidates. Switched the staleness criterion from last-commit date to the GitHub Issues API `closed_at` field. Also added `bin/audit-tests-common.sh` (orphan detection for scope:common tests whose `# Tests:` paths all no longer exist), `bin/check-test-scope-tag.sh` (scope tag enforcement with `--staged` pre-commit and `--all` manual sweep modes), two nightly CI steps in `sweep.yml`, a new `/sweep-tests` sub-skill, and wired it into the `/sweep` hub as SW-4. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/1557-audit-tests-retire pr=#1562 -->

### FEATURE: PR #1577 — fix/1568-enforce-worktree-false-positives (2026-07-20, 82816a9, #1577)
Background: fix(#1568 #1533 #1457 #1449 #1385 #1191): fix 6 false positives in enforce-worktree hook
Changes: #1568: `isNewlineInjectedWriteIR` false-positive on `gh issue create --body "$(multiline)"` from main — DQ body with literal newlines was split as separate commands; fixed by applying `stripDqPreservingCmdSubst` before the newline split in `bash-write-targets.js`, folding DQ literal content to `""` while preserving `$()` regions.;#1533: `ISSUE_CREATE_SKILL=1 bash dispatch.sh --body "$(multiline)"` blocked from main — argTail guard's `\n` scan fired on literal newlines inside DQ body arg; fixed by applying `foldDqNewlines` helper in `worker-script.js` to collapse DQ-internal newlines to spaces before the argTail regex scan, while leaving `$()` unchanged (fail-closed preserved).;#1457: `gh issue create --body $'it'\''s fine'` blocked — `hasUnclosedQuote` in `command-ir.js` didn't recognize ANSI-C `$'...'` spans or backslash-escape in unquoted context (`\'`), causing `parseFailure:true` and write classification; fixed by adding an ANSI-C branch and a `\x`-skip in the unquoted else branch.;#1449: `bash skills/review-code-security/scripts/run-quality-gates.sh` blocked from main — the SANCTIONED allow-list in `worker-script.js` did not include this path; fixed by adding it alongside the other existing sanctioned scripts.;#1385: `bash -c 'node bin/workflow/read-complexity-evaluation --session sid'` blocked from main — no predicate allowed read-only `bash -c '...'` workflow CLI calls; fixed by adding `isAllowedReadOnlyWorkflowCli` in `standard.js` (delegates to `isReadOnlyInterpreterC`) and wiring it into the allow-chain in `enforce-worktree.js`.;#1191: `ISSUE_CREATE_SKILL=1 bash "dispatch.sh"` blocked from main when prefixed with a VAR=val env assignment — the identity regex in `worker-script.js` required the command to start with `bash`; extended the regex to accept one or more `VAR=val` prefixes before `bash`. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/1568-enforce-worktree-false-positives pr=#1577 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1578 — fix/propagate-labels-fixes (2026-07-20, 6ef66755002cfa1b23659a23e3095605142e37a2, #1578)
Background: fix(#1560,#1546,#1262,#1545,#1548,#1565): propagate-labels PAT fallback, depth-1 scan, hooksPath clear, asset copy, docs, fixture fixes
