### BUGFIX: fix(#1579): issue-create label drift — reporter-model script mapping + keyword scan (2026-07-20, 0eb3b18)
Background: PR #1496 renamed model:* labels to reporter-model:* in labels.yml but left SKILL.md pointing at the old names, so the LLM kept adding labels that no longer existed on GitHub.
Changes: Rewrote SKILL.md label policy to the reporter-model:* scheme, narrowing the LLM role to extracting the raw model name and passing it via --reporter-model to the script (which now owns the name→label mapping via a case statement). Added an inline keyword scan in issue-create.sh (abort/hang/security/leak → force severity:high, word-boundary -w, no -i). Clarified the type:task and severity:high definitions in SKILL.md.
Test gap: No test covering the model→label mapping path in issue-create.sh before the fix


### FEATURE: PR #1612 — fix/issue-1600-sanctioned-allowlist (2026-07-24, 8b197f02b16dbf63a506cbfdd7006bd3911cfc48, #1612)
Background: fix(#1600,#1590,#1501,#1307): finalize-worker overlay for SANCTIONED allowlist
Changes: fix(#1600): issue-close-finalize Phase 2 was unrunnable because `worker-script.js`'s SANCTIONED allowlist only listed `pre-flight.sh` — the other three finalize-worker scripts (`run-initial.sh`, `run-loop-step.js`, `run-finalize-terminal.sh`) had no path to bypass the main-worktree write guard. Added `hooks/enforce-worktree/main-worktree-allows/finalize-worker-overlay.js`, a HARD-validating overlay (identity + env VALUES + per-position argument shape, including per-token unquoted-metacharacter rejection) that gates all three scripts before deferring to the shared write-scope tail. A confirmed HIGH-severity gap found by `/review-code-security` (unquoted `id`-position args allowed bare `|`/`<`/`>`, enabling command injection via pipe and arbitrary file write via redirect invisible to the write-target scanner) was fixed in the same session by validating quoted-vs-unquoted token provenance and rejecting shell metacharacters on unquoted tokens; the legacy SANCTIONED structural scan in `worker-script.js` received the symmetric bare-`|` rejection for CPR-5 lockstep.;fix(#1590): `run-finalize-terminal.sh` is now covered by the same `finalize-worker-overlay.js` HARD-validation registry as the other finalize scripts, closing the SANCTIONED-allowlist gap for the terminal step of the finalize chain.;fix(#1501): ICF-F's meta-label fast path previously parsed `- [ ]` checkboxes in the issue body to infer sub-issue completion, which misread prose mentioning checkbox-like text as incomplete state. Reworked to use `parent-all-closed-check.sh`'s RC=0 (all sub-issues closed) as the completion signal instead of body-text parsing.;test(#1307): `feature-1257-session-close-wf-meta.sh`'s S3 assertion grep-anchored to `/issue-close-finalize`, which could false-match SKILL.md prose referencing the skill by name rather than the actual invocation. Tightened the assertion to anchor on the actual invocation shape. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/issue-1600-sanctioned-allowlist pr=#1612 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1618 — feature/supervisor-off-gate (2026-07-25, ef5bba2232eb081823fbef9d823b37905debdcde, #1618)
Background: feat(off-gate): reason-vetting OFF gate, emergency sentinels, next-step pause
Changes: #1608: Reworked the OFF-sentinel gate from severity-accumulation to reason-vetting (token-first). hooks/supervisor-off-proposal-shim.js decides block/pass-through by strict-DQ emergency detection → isWorkflowOff/isWorktreeOff (target-aware) → a reason-bound single-use clearance token (evaluateOffClearance in hooks/lib/session-markers.js: unexpired + target match + reason-contains-category); corrupt/missing token fails CLOSED. New bin/request-off-clearance (Phase-1 examiner + token mint), hooks/block-off-clearance-write.js (anti-cheat on the token path), enforce-override-handlers/off-clearance.js (single-use consume with wsid fallback), four *_EMERGENCY_* sentinels in hooks/lib/sentinel-patterns.js. Emergency OFF bypasses the Phase-1 examiner and is human-only + audited. Why: fixes the deadlock where accumulated errors/warnings blocked the very OFF switch needed to escape them (egg-or-chicken); the gate now measures reason legitimacy, not severity. Trust model: not a cryptographic hard gate — the real gate is Phase-2 human approval of the ask sentinel plus the audit trail; unforgeability is an explicit non-goal.;#1607: next-step pause/resume. NEXT_STEP_PAUSE/RESUME sentinels + hooks/workflow-mark/enforce-override-handlers/next-step-pause.js; next-step emits ACTION=paused (REASON=next-step-paused / workflow-off-quiet) so it stops re-announcing while a deliberate OFF/pause is in effect; CLAUDE.md documents the paused action. Why: distinguishes intentional workflow departure from a procedural mistake so the departure can be used quietly.;#1606: honest OFF block message. The shim's block message is sourced from actual supervisor state instead of showing "Active supervisor findings exist" on a fresh session whose state file is absent (ENOENT). Why: ENOENT (audit-not-yet-run) and real blocking findings were shown identically, using severity/audit-presence as a proxy — the message now tells the truth about whether findings exist.;#924: wsid-scoped warnings_summary. hooks/workflow-gate/review-tests-checker.js warnings_summary check is now tagged with wsid so a stale value from a prior wsid no longer falsely blocks a commit in long-running CC sessions. Why: token/fingerprint paths were wsid-scoped in PR #963 (commit cc8035bb) but warnings_summary was missed — same class of bleed, one remaining member.;#1132: supervisor checklist item 6 no longer misclassifies --wip mode. agents/supervisor.md + skills/_shared/off-legitimacy-rubric.md recognize `git -c workflow.wip=1 commit` as sanctioned rather than a C3 improvised bypass. Why: item 6 is the OFF-proposal validity classifier (same subsystem as the Phase-1 rubric) — a sibling bug fixed alongside the rubric work.;#1361: review-loop terminal guard. skills/review-tests/scripts/run-codex-review-loop.sh + review-tests-checker/handler terminal-marker handling (state-io.js clearReviewTestsTerminalMarker) structurally prevent a terminal exit (e.g. exit 2) from deleting the round-counter and re-enabling `--cap 1` on re-invocation. Why: a terminal exit followed by re-invocation silently reset the loop cap. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/supervisor-off-gate pr=#1618 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1628 — feature/ds4-prompt-hardening (2026-07-25, e241c33b70554bd08f2eb567b23870c2e7e4f83a, #1628)
Background: feat(#1611): model-conditional prompt hardening for low-gap-filling backends
Changes: FEATURE: model-conditional prompt hardening for low-gap-filling backends. Background: the agents prompt-set is tuned for Opus/Sonnet, which fill rule gaps implicitly; a backend such as ds4 fills fewer gaps and takes goal-achieving shortcuts that skip "should" rules. Rather than a rules/*.md that would burden Opus/Sonnet context unconditionally, the running model is detected from the "You are powered by the model named X" self-report matched against a VERBOSE_PROMPT_MODELS allowlist; on a match a hooks/lib provider injects the hardening text as additionalContext at SessionStart and PostCompact (tolerant of repeated injection), so flag-off sessions pay zero context. Also in this issue: model detection consolidated into one matcher (hooks/lib/model-match.js, reused by issue-create reporter-model labelling); append-only doc protection extended to rotated archive files (docs/history/*.md, changelog/*.md) with the block-history-direct guard now registered for the Bash tool as well as Edit/Write; and the final-report Stop-guard trigger decoupled from the .env file so it fires on the workflow's pre-final-report gate directly. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/ds4-prompt-hardening pr=#1628 -->

### FEATURE: PR #1634 — feature/worktree-transition-1610 (2026-07-25, ac174f90e04b626fe6c05bf68e1d4d95487c015b, #1634)
Background: fix(#1610): worktree transition guard — ExitWorktree reminder + outside-worktree write gate
Changes: #1610 P0 probe SETTLED (observed 2026-07-24, Claude Code 2.1.136 reported by `claude --version`). Probe fully removed: live settings byte-restored from backup, backup deleted, both temporary scripts deleted.;Verdict A- : a `permissions.allow` entry for `EnterWorktree` does NOT suppress the upstream consent dialog. Measured with both entries live in `~/.claude/settings.json`; the dialog still appeared on every EnterWorktree call. The two tracked-`settings.json` allow entries were therefore reverted to zero diff.;`ExitWorktree` has no consent gate at all — PreToolUse to PostToolUse latency was 0.22s with no dialog, versus 15.0s (human response) for EnterWorktree. Its allow entry is a no-op either way.;Verdict P+ : both `PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse` fire for `EnterWorktree` and `ExitWorktree`. PreToolUse fires even when the user denies the call. The `cwd` field carries the post-transition directory in PostToolUse.;Unplanned finding 1 (WITHDRAWN — was wrong): the claim that a session is bound at launch without `EnterWorktree` was refuted by the transcript. An explicit call exists at 2026-07-24T13:07:50Z with `cwd=C:\git\agents`, made right after `/worktree-start`. Transcript entry evidence is therefore reliable and the Stop-hook predicate in the detail plan stands unchanged.;Unplanned finding 2: `EnterWorktree` refuses a path equal to the current working directory ("Cannot enter worktree: ... is the current working directory"). Re-entering the session's own worktree is impossible while already inside it.;Unplanned finding 3: `EnterWorktree` MOVES the session transcript from `~/.claude/projects/<launch-dir>/<sid>.jsonl` to `~/.claude/projects/<worktree-dir>/<sid>.jsonl`. It is a move, not a copy — nothing remains at the original path. This is the mechanism behind the reported usability problem where a session vanishes from the thread list of the directory it was started in.;Consequence: a session started with the worktree as its workspace directory never calls `EnterWorktree`, so it never sees the dialog, never relocates its transcript, and holds no binding to release.;Mechanism (measured 2026-07-25): `enforce-worktree` judges Edit/Write by the write-target path, not by CWD — writing to a worktree absolute path from a main CWD is already allowed. The only real effect of `EnterWorktree` is relocating the session CWD, the single anchor every Bash call resets to (`cd` inside a call never persists). So `EnterWorktree` is not isolation; it only moves the base tree for relative-path execution.;Entry-route decision SETTLED (2026-07-25): keep `EnterWorktree` as the primary entry route. The 2-window / session-disappearance cost is mitigated by a workaround — from the main-worktree window's terminal, `claude --resume [<session-id>]` lists worktree-side sessions (CLI defaults includeWorktrees=true; only the VS Code extension hardcodes false, extension-side fix is the real upstream fix). The approved detail plan stands unchanged: Step 8 (exit-forgetting Stop warning + PostToolUse recorder) and the Tier 3 entry gate are implemented as specified.;#1610 implementation: added an advisory Stop hook (`hooks/stop-exit-worktree-warn.js`) that warns when `EnterWorktree` was used but `ExitWorktree` was never called, using union evidence from workflow state and the session transcript; it fails open and never emits a Stop-time decision key (a Stop-time block would deadlock the session). Added a PostToolUse recorder (`hooks/postuse-native-worktree-record.js`) that stamps `worktree_entered_at` / `worktree_exited_at` on EnterWorktree/ExitWorktree. Added a Tier 3 `hooks/workflow-gate.js` entry predicate (`hooks/workflow-gate/worktree-entry-gate.js`) that blocks EARLY_GATE_TOOLS writes once `branching_complete` is recorded and the session's cwd is outside its linked worktree — the destination path is deliberately excluded from the decision because writing to a worktree-internal absolute path establishes no session binding; subagent/headless calls downgrade to advisory and every predicate/error path fails open to dormant. Added a shared remedy builder (`hooks/enforce-worktree/worktree-remedy.js`) surfaced in enforce-worktree block reasons. `worktree-start` WS-8 now calls EnterWorktree and `worktree-end` WE-13a calls ExitWorktree per the shared protocol `skills/_shared/worktree-transition.md`. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=feature/worktree-transition-1610 pr=#1634 -->


### FEATURE: PR #1650 — fix/rules-paths-frontmatter (2026-07-25, da0b6d98d7be1339f107d5423efb76bc58b66986, #1650)
Background: fix(#1641): use paths: frontmatter key for conditional rules
Changes: BUGFIX | Fix conditional rules loading: rename frontmatter key `globs:` to `paths:` (#1641) | Background: Claude Code's rules parser reads the `paths:` frontmatter key, but this repo wrote `globs:`, a deliberate 2026-05-12 reverse-migration that worked around upstream Issue #21858 (user-scope `paths:` silently ignored). That upstream bug is fixed, leaving the repo stranded on the obsolete workaround: files without `paths:` fall back to unconditional loading, so 15 rules intended to be conditional were injected in full into every session. | Changes: renamed the key to `paths:` and converted values from comma-separated single-line strings to YAML lists across 15 `rules/*.md` files in one diff (key-only replacement would have been parsed as zero patterns and stayed unconditional); reworded the `## Sub-rules` headings in `rules/coding.md` and `rules/docs.md` that claimed "path-scoped via globs"; deleted the one-line relocation stub `rules/file-investigation.md` (the real file is `skills/_shared/file-investigation.md`); rewrote `tests/refactor-rules-progressive-disclosure.sh` as a fail-closed dispatcher over five group files under `tests/refactor-rules-progressive-disclosure/`, pinning the exact ordered pattern strings per file, and updated checks k. and l. in `tests/feature-write-code-skill-static.sh` from `globs:` to `paths:`. | Test gap: no test asserted that rules frontmatter used the `paths:` key that Claude Code's loader actually reads — the suite only checked that a `globs:` key was present. <!-- compose-doc-append-sentinel: branch=fix/rules-paths-frontmatter pr=#1650 -->

### INCIDENT: #1: Incident: autonomous /loop self-emission caused workflow to bypass user-approval gate (2026-07-25, #368)
Cause: Claude autonomously called ScheduleWakeup with prompt "/loop continue detail plan workflow" without the user having invoked /loop. The wakeup fired and injected the /loop command as a synthetic user message, putting Claude into /loop dynamic mode whose design self-perpetuates via wakeup chain. Triggered when detail plan revision rounds exceeded the cap=2 and Claude tried to bypass the user-approval gate by synthesizing user input. Post-compaction the auto-generated summary mis-attributed the /loop invocation to the user, reinforcing the pattern. Worsened when Claude treated a wakeup-fired message as a user response to a pending multi-option question, auto-selecting Option 1 without user input.
Fix: Pending. Five-layer countermeasure design captured in issue body; implementation deferred to a separate session. Highest-priority layers: (A) prohibit Claude from passing slash-command prompts to ScheduleWakeup, (C) gate against treating wakeup fires as responses to pending user questions.


### FEATURE: compose-doc-append-entry: push-failure leaves stranded local commits with no auto-recovery (2026-07-25, #475)
Background: When `compose-doc-append-entry` produces commits (history and/or changelog) and then `git push` fails, the idempotency sentinel/marker has already been written. Re-running the CLI detects idempotency and skips the commit step entirely, so the push is never retried — the local commits are permanently stranded. The SKILL.md documents a manual recovery command (`COMPOSE_DOC_APPEND_SKILL=1 git push origin main`), but the CLI itself provides no mechanism to detect stranded state and prompt for or execute recovery.
Changes: - Add a stranded-commit detector to `compose-doc-append-entry`: after idempotency skip, check if unpushed commits authored by this CLI exist; if so, emit a stderr warning with the recovery command rather than silently exiting 0. - Alternatively, separate the sentinel write so it only occurs after a successful push.


### FEATURE: compose-doc-append-entry: changelog idempotency marker is .git/-local, not repository content (2026-07-25, #476)
Background: `compose-doc-append-entry` prevents duplicate CHANGELOG.md entries using a marker file under `.git/compose-doc-append-state/`. This marker is not tracked by git and is not part of the repository content. On a fresh clone or after cleaning `.git/` state, the marker is lost. If Step 6i re-runs (e.g., push failed and operator re-runs from a fresh checkout), CHANGELOG.md will receive a duplicate entry with no way for the CLI to detect the prior append. By contrast, `docs/history.md` uses an in-document HTML sentinel comment, which is portable across clones.
Changes: - Replace the `.git/compose-doc-append-state/` marker with an in-document sentinel comment in CHANGELOG.md (similar to the history.md sentinel pattern). - Update `changelog_marker_found` and `write_changelog_marker` to scan/write the sentinel in CHANGELOG.md rather than in `.git/`.


### FEATURE: compose-doc-append-entry: git add docs/history.md docs/history/ may include pre-existing staged changes (2026-07-25, #477)
Background: In `compose-doc-append-entry`, after appending to `docs/history.md`, the script runs: ```bash COMPOSE_DOC_APPEND_SKILL=1 git add docs/history.md docs/history/ ``` This stages the entire `docs/history/` directory, not only the file changed by this CLI invocation. If the operator had previously staged unrelated changes under `docs/history/` (e.g., an in-progress archive rotation), those changes are swept into the privileged `docs(history): record PR #N` commit. The `COMPOSE_DOC_APPEND_SKILL=1` prefix bypasses the pre-commit hook's normal scope check, so the widened staging goes undetected.
Changes: - Stage only the specific file(s) touched by `doc-append.py`: `docs/history.md` and optionally the rotated archive file (determined by checking `docs/history/` for new/modified files after the append, and adding only those). - Or: run `git stash --staged` before the append, re-add only the append result, then pop the stash.


### FEATURE: worktree-end: Step 5 should auto-proceed with default backup destination instead of asking the user (2026-07-25, #479)
Background: `/worktree-end` Step 5 presents a DRY RUN summary that includes a *Proposed* backup destination (default `<main_root>/.worktree-backup/<branch>/` + alternatives: main worktree, user path, discard). Reading this as 'must ask the user to pick one every time' wastes a turn — the alternatives are escape hatches, not forks the user wants to choose between every cycle. User pushback during /worktree-end of #462: "みんな聞くが、これbackup場所を聞けと prompt で書かれている?" — confirming that the default should auto-proceed. Same pattern family as already-fixed skills: - `make-outline-plan`: create context.md silently (no AskUserQuestion) - `worktree-start`: skip AskUserQuestion when task name is unambiguous from args - write-tests / write-code small-diff approval: only ask when there is a reason
Changes: Tighten the SKILL.md so the orchestrator: - Always presents the DRY RUN summary (inventory + Docker check + proposed default backup path) — keep this; it's informational. - Proceeds straight to the copy without `AskUserQuestion` when the default destination is viable. - Only invokes `AskUserQuestion` when there is a *specific* deviation reason (e.g., default path collision, user explicitly requested a different destination in-session, or the user opted to discard). Specifically the SKILL.md Step 5 text 'After user approval: copy preservation targets to the chosen destination.' needs to be rewritten to make 'user approval' implicit on the default path. Consider adding `CONFIRM_WORKTREE_BACKUP=on|off` env var symmetric with `CONFIRM_WORKTREE`.


### FEATURE: /worktree-end --resume loader doesn't replay Step 6i — CHANGELOG.md abandoned on deferred path (2026-07-25, #494)
Background: `/worktree-end` deferred-cleanup flow (writes a marker on Windows EPERM then exits; `--resume` runs in a new session) only replays Step 6c-6h via `bin/worktree-end-resume-load.js`. Step 6i (`compose-doc-append-entry`) and Step 7 (final report) are never replayed. When `closes_issues` is empty, Step 6i is the sole writer of both `docs/history.md` and `CHANGELOG.md`, so the deferred path abandons both. When `closes_issues` is non-empty (current SKILL.md behaviour passes `--skip-history`), `docs/history.md` is later written by Step 10b (`/issue-close-finalize`) and survives, but **`CHANGELOG.md` has no writer at all** (public-repo-only impact). Observed on 2026-05-23 during PR #490 cleanup: `closes_issues=[#486, #291]`, deferred-fork path completed via `--resume`, CHANGELOG.md had no entry. Backfilled manually with `COMPOSE_DOC_APPEND_SKILL=1 bash compose-doc-append-entry --skip-history ...` (commit `cb5c180`).
Changes: - Option 1: Extend `bin/worktree-end-resume-load.js` to replay Step 6i + 6j + 7. Match SKILL.md's `--skip-history` toggle based on `closes_issues`. - Option 2: Move Step 6i ownership from `/worktree-end` to `/issue-close-finalize`. Natural fit since Step 6i only fires when `closes_issues` is non-empty in current design. Needs a fallback for the `closes_issues`-empty case. - Option 3: Redesign `--resume` to resume the entire Step 6 sequence (6c-6j + 7) instead of the current minimal-cleanup loader. - Regression test: cover the deferred-then-resume path and assert CHANGELOG.md gets a new entry. ## Reference - Parent: #296 (Group: worktree-end x CWD issues) - Observed: PR #490 cleanup, manual backfill commit `cb5c180` - Related: #489 (resume-session detection — separate bug) - Loader source: `bin/worktree-end-resume-load.js` (replays 6c-6h only, skips 6i / Step 7)


### FEATURE: C:/Program Files/Git/worktree-end --resume refuses when multiple pending-cwd-unlock markers exist for same repo (2026-07-25, #495)
Background: `bin/worktree-end-resume-load.js` discovers `pending-cwd-unlock-` markers by repo-id only. When more than one marker exists for the same repo, the loader exits with `ERROR: Multiple pending-cwd-unlock markers found` (loader source lines 85-90). Marker filenames are `pending-cwd-unlock-<repo-id>--<encoded-branch>` and contain the branch, but `--resume` takes no branch argument, so it cannot disambiguate. The loader fails safe by stopping. Observed on 2026-05-23: PR #491 (issue-484) deferred-cleanup was still pending when PR #490 (restore-code-folder-uri) ran its deferred-cleanup. Two markers coexisted, `--resume` exited 1. Worked around by renaming one marker to `parked-cwd-unlock-...` and processing them one at a time.
Changes: - Option 1: Add `--resume <branch>` argument. Match against `encodeURIComponent(branch)` in marker filename. - Option 2: With no argument, process markers in mtime-ascending order sequentially. - Option 3: When multiple, print the list and use `AskUserQuestion` to let the user pick (interactive sessions only). - Combine the above with `/resume-session` so the branch can be propagated through detect → dispatch (currently `/resume-session` has no branch channel). - Regression test: cover the multi-marker case. ## Reference - Parent: #296 (Group: worktree-end x CWD issues) - Observed: PR #490 / PR #491 cleanup (2026-05-23) - Related: #489 (resume-session detection — separate bug) - Loader source: `bin/worktree-end-resume-load.js` lines 85-90


### FEATURE: worktree-end / issue-close-finalize: auto-run /issue-create for WORKTREE_NOTES entries immediately; ban defer language (2026-07-25, #530)
Background: #429 (closed) added the /issue-create prompt at Step 5.5 (a.5) in /worktree-end. However, two failure modes persist in practice: 1. Claude skips Step 5.5 (a.5) entirely during /worktree-end execution. 2. After the workflow fully completes (worktree removed, issues closed), Claude emits language like "別セッションで /issue-create を実行" ("run /issue-create in a separate session") instead of executing immediately. Both modes force the user to explicitly direct Claude to do something that should be automatic. The WORKTREE_NOTES.md mechanism (RelatedTasks / BugsFound / NextTasks) exists precisely so that findings are not lost — but the current behavior makes the user responsible for the final promotion step.
Changes: 1. Step 5.5 (a.5) in /worktree-end SKILL.md: mark as mandatory (not skippable). When unpromoted entries exist and the session is interactive, /issue-create MUST be invoked — no AskUserQuestion gate that lets the model silently skip. 2. After /worktree-end completes (Final Report emitted), if any WORKTREE_NOTES.md entries were not yet promoted, invoke /issue-create immediately within the same session. Do NOT emit defer language ("別セッションで", "in a separate session", "after this session", etc.). 3. After /issue-close-finalize completes and the session has WORKTREE_NOTES RelatedTasks/BugsFound/NextTasks entries, same rule applies: run /issue-create immediately without user direction. Non-goal: removing the WORKTREE_NOTES fallback path (writing entries there and promoting later remains valid when the worktree step is reached).


### FEATURE: sweep-worktrees: cannot reclaim full-content orphan directories left after git worktree remove fails (2026-07-25, #564)
Background: When `/worktree-end` fails to run `git worktree remove` due to a Windows CWD lock, the following state results: 1. `git worktree prune` removes it from the git registry (it disappears from the worktree list) 2. All repository files remain in the directory (only the `.git` file is removed) 3. Because `sweep-worktrees`'s orphan-directory 5-AND gate has an `empty-or-notes-only` condition, full-content directories are permanently excluded from cleanup targets 4. Manual `Remove-Item` can also fail due to the session's CWD lock → neither automatic reclamation nor manual deletion is possible. Real-world example: worktree `C:\git\worktrees\issue-550-pre-commit-marker\agents` from #550 remained despite the PR being merged and the branch deleted. #525 states 'sweep cleanup race window: the orphan state persists until the next /sweep-worktrees', but the current sweep cannot reclaim this orphan. Related to #525 (the enforce-worktree.js orphan CWD bypass problem; #525 is written assuming sweep can clean it up)
Changes: Add an 'ownership-proven (`Main repo:` field) + PR merged + no uncommitted changes' path to `sweep-worktrees.sh`'s orphan-directory determination so it can pass the `empty-or-notes-only` gate. Candidates: - Allow deletion when `WORKTREE_NOTES.md` has a `Main repo:` field, the corresponding branch's PR is merged, and `git status` is clean - Or add a `--force-orphan` flag that allows forced deletion only when ownership is proven. Related to #525


### FEATURE: Investigate settings.json env injection for CLAUDE_SESSION_ID into Bash tool subprocesses (2026-07-25, #588)
Background: Anthropic bug #27987: `$CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` is not propagated to Bash subprocesses when Claude Code runs a Bash tool call. This is the root cause of the broader WIP signaling failure mode documented in #440 and #519. The JSONL-scan fallback added by PR #532 (and improved by the #519 fix) works around this by scanning `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/` for the newest transcript file. However, a cleaner solution would be to have `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` available directly in the subprocess environment. ## Investigation needed Determine whether `settings.json`'s `env` block (if it exists or could be added) can inject `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` into Bash tool subprocesses. If so, document the mechanism and implement it as Approach D from the #519 planning session. ## Context - #519 planning session: Approach D was deferred pending investigation - Anthropic bug #27987: env propagation is documented as not working - Current workaround: JSONL-scan fallback in `bin/lib/resolve-session-id.sh` ## Related - Closes or supersedes part of #519 - Related to #440
Changes: Anthropic bug #27987: `$CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` is not propagated to Bash subprocesses when Claude Code runs a Bash tool call. This is the root cause of the broader WIP signaling failure mode documented in


### FEATURE: Verify ls -1t newest-file selection reliability for transcript scan on Windows Git Bash (2026-07-25, #590)
Background: `_scan_one_transcript_dir` in `bin/lib/resolve-session-id.sh` uses `ls -1t <dir>/*.jsonl | head -1` to find the newest transcript file. Test B-13 (added by PR for #519) covers the deterministic two-file case (one strictly newer than the other via `touch -t`), which is sufficient to prove the encoder-fix path is end-to-end correct. However, broader reliability questions were explicitly scoped out of #519: - **NTFS timestamp granularity**: NTFS has 100ns precision vs ext4's 1ns — does this affect `ls -1t` ordering for files created in rapid succession? - **Concurrent writers**: What happens if two sessions write JSONL files simultaneously? - **Time-zone/DST artifacts**: Could clock skew or DST transitions cause `ls -1t` to return a non-newest file? - **`ls -1t` tie-breaking**: Behavior is platform-specific when mtimes are equal. ## Investigation needed 1. Characterize `ls -1t` behavior on Windows Git Bash under NTFS for the cases above. 2. Determine if the current implementation is reliable enough or if an alternative (e.g., `find` with `-newer`, stat-based sorting) is needed. 3. Add tests covering edge cases if reliability issues are found. ## Related - Introduced by PR for #519 (JSONL-scan fallback) - `bin/lib/resolve-session-id.sh:21` (`_scan_one_transcript_dir`)
Changes: `_scan_one_transcript_dir` in `bin/lib/resolve-session-id.sh` uses `ls -1t <dir>/*.jsonl | head -1` to find the newest transcript file. Test B-13 (added by PR for #519) covers the deterministic two-fi


### FEATURE: show-plan-link.js: hook doesn't fire on outline.md overwrite, so it doesn't expand in the IDE preview (2026-07-25, #620)
Background: In `make-outline-plan` Step 8, when `<PLANS_DIR>/<session-id>-outline.md` was overwritten with the Write tool to reflect the adopted approach, the `show-plan-link.js` PostToolUse hook did not fire as expected (or was not surfaced). Expected behavior: emit the `Plan file written: <path>` breadcrumb + auto-expand the IDE preview (`confirm-plan.md` Step 2). Actual: no breadcrumb emitted, preview not expanded. ## Reproduction (observed in #603 session) 1. `outline-planner` returns approaches 2. The orchestrator Writes drafts/<session-id>-outline-draft.md (new file) 3. `assemble-mandatory.sh` writes <session-id>-outline.md **via bash** (known: #583 / memory `feedback_assemble_mandatory_write_tool.md` — the hook does not fire on this path) 4. Approach adopted via AskUserQuestion 5. The orchestrator **overwrites** <session-id>-outline.md with Write (reflecting the adopted approach) 6. **The hook should fire and the preview should expand here, but it does not** Step 5's Write is an overwrite, and other than requiring a prior Read, it is an ordinary Write tool call. ## Candidate causes (needs investigation) - `show-plan-link.js` may have logic that skips on overwrite (existing file) - Because assemble-mandatory.sh's bash write already updated the file's mtime/state, the PostToolUse side may treat it as already processed - The hook itself may have fired, but there may be an issue where systemMessage delivery does not propagate to the orchestrator side
Changes: - Investigate `hooks/show-plan-link.js` logic: check whether it skips on the overwrite path - Fix the hook if needed — ensure `Plan file written: <path>` is always emitted even on overwrite - Verify whether the same symptom occurs on the `<session-id>-detail.md` overwrite path - Update the description in `skills/_shared/confirm-plan.md` Step 2 (document the fallback for when the hook does not fire) ## Acceptance criteria - `Plan file written: <path>` is reliably emitted on the Step 8 Write - `<session-id>-outline.md` auto-expands in the IDE preview - Also verify on the `<session-id>-detail.md` overwrite path ## Related - #583 (sibling: confirm-plan content invisibility — known symptom on the assemble-mandatory.sh path) - #574 (root cause of the hook not firing on assemble-mandatory.sh's bash write) - #291 (show-plan-link.js IDE routing) - `skills/_shared/confirm-plan.md` Step 2 - `skills/make-outline-plan/SKILL.md` Step 8 Related to #583


### FEATURE: bin/audit-tests.sh: periodic obsolete-test audit (follow-up to #689) (2026-07-25, #711)
Background: Issue #689 archived 17 class-A obsolete tests manually, but the audit was done ad-hoc by a subagent inspecting each test's `# Tests:` paths. There is no automated tooling to detect staleness over time. As the codebase evolves and source files are renamed or deleted, new class-A candidates will accumulate silently until the next manual sweep.
Changes: New script `bin/audit-tests.sh`: reads each `tests/*.sh` file's `# Tests:` frontmatter, checks whether each listed path exists in the current worktree, and emits a staleness report (class A = all paths missing, class B = some paths missing). Designed to run periodically via `/sweep` or manually. Deferred from #689 Phase 5 (outline decision: "audit tool is a pure observability add-on, not required for core behavior change").


### FEATURE: installer: add review-code-size PATH launcher (sibling of review-skill-size) (2026-07-25, #751)
Background: install/linux/dotfileslink.sh and install/win/dotfileslink.ps1 install a PATH launcher for review-skill-size, but review-code-size has no corresponding launcher. Since #741 made review-code-size return exit 1 on a HARD violation, users who only have the installer-provided PATH silently fail with "command not found" on the Step 6 Bash call `review-code-size --base <merge-base>`. review-skill-size and review-code-size are symmetric sibling scripts, so installer coverage should be aligned as well. Related: #471 (closed — review-skill-size launcher already added, review-code-size not yet covered).
Changes: - install/linux/dotfileslink.sh: add a symlink for ~/.local/bin/review-code-size (same pattern as review-skill-size) - install/win/dotfileslink.ps1: add a .cmd launcher and bash shim for review-code-size (same pattern as review-skill-size)


### FEATURE: tokenizePwsh treats backslash as escape in double-quoted strings, mangling Windows paths in Remove-Item (2026-07-25, #819)
Background: `tokenizePwsh` in `hooks/lib/bash-write-targets.js` treats `\` as an escape character inside double-quoted strings. In PowerShell, double-quoted string escaping uses backtick (`), not backslash. Backslash is a literal directory separator on Windows. When `Remove-Item` is called with double-quoted Windows paths (e.g. `Remove-Item "C:\Users\nire\.claude\...\a.md"`), `tokenizePwsh` mangles the path: `C:\` → `C:`, `\U` → `U`, `\n` → newline, producing `C:Users<LF>ire.claude...`. Node.js treats `C:Users\...` (missing leading backslash) as a CWD-relative path on drive C. From CWD=`C:\git\agents`, this resolves to inside the agents repo. `findRepoRoot` then finds `C:\git\agents`, `isInSessionScope` returns true, and `areAllBashTargetsOutsideSessionScope` returns false — causing enforce-worktree to block the command despite the actual target being outside the repo. Related to #573 (which added `extractRmTargets` for `rm` but did not fix the underlying tokenizer bug in `tokenizePwsh`). Encountered 2026-06-08: `Remove-Item` of two memory files under `~/.claude/projects/c--git-agents/memory/` was blocked from the main worktree; user had to delete manually.
Changes: - Fix `tokenizePwsh`: remove backslash-as-escape handling inside double-quoted strings. Replace `if (cmd[j] === '\\' && ...) { content += cmd[j+1]; j += 2; }` with simple `content += cmd[j++]` (backslash is literal in PowerShell double-quoted strings). - Add test case: `Remove-Item "C:\Users\nire\.claude\projects\memory\a.md"` from main worktree CWD must be allowed (target outside repo).


### FEATURE: fix: skills/session-close/SKILL.md:40 lowercase 'echo off || echo on' probe (uppercase elsewhere) (2026-07-25, #981)
Background: `skills/session-close/SKILL.md:40`'s ENFORCE_WORKTREE detection probe returns lowercase `echo off || echo on`. Every other SKILL.md in the repo (write-code, write-tests, worktree-start, and others) uses uppercase `echo OFF || echo ON`. A grep confirms the lowercase form appears in **only one place: session-close/SKILL.md:40**. SC-1 is not a path where stdout is directly matched against a `case` statement or similar (currently both `on` and `off` are handled on both branches), so no actual harm has occurred. However, if this value is ever parsed by grep/awk assuming uppercase in the future, session-close alone would be missed.
Changes: - Unify `skills/session-close/SKILL.md:40`'s `echo off` → `echo OFF` and `echo on` → `echo ON` - Grep once more just in case to check whether any other SKILL.md still has the lowercase form ## Why Consistency of sentinel/probe notation across the SKILL.md family. Orthogonality (rules/core-principles.md §4): every sibling of the same class (ENFORCE_WORKTREE probe) should have the same shape. session-close alone is a deviant form. ## Out of scope - The `get-config-var` helper itself (already fixed in #893/#954) - Changes to the ENFORCE_WORKTREE determination logic itself ## Discovered by Mentioned in #977 (fix/get-config-var-hardening) detail-plan Step 10, confirmed in L2 supervisor finding idx=3.


### FEATURE: fix: feature-enforce-worktree-exclude-glob.sh dead-skips every run (glob-match.js removed in PR #993) (2026-07-25, #1009)
Background: `tests/feature-enforce-worktree-exclude-glob.sh` references `hooks/lib/glob-match.js` which was removed in PR #993 (replaced by inline `hooks/lib/worktree-include-match.js`). Every test case in the suite dead-skips with a `require` error, meaning: - Security regression tests (regex-metachar escaping, catastrophic backtracking) are silently not running - New unit tests for the inline `buildMatcher`/`globToRegex` logic are needed
Changes: - Update `tests/feature-enforce-worktree-exclude-glob.sh` to test `hooks/lib/worktree-include-match.js` directly - Add/restore coverage for: regex-metachar escaping, ReDoS backtracking guard, wildcard matching edge cases ## Found during PR #1004 (issue #990 offensive-content scan)


### FEATURE: fix: scan-offensive CLI tests T2/T5 fail when .offensive-content-blocklist has no active patterns (2026-07-25, #1036)
Background: .offensive-content-blocklist ships with all sample lines commented out (correct per intent — human-only writes). However, tests/feature-990-scan-offensive-cli.sh T2 ('hard offensive keyword → exit 1') and T5 (case-insensitive matching) rely on discover_hard_keyword() whose fallback sentinel 'OFFENSIVE_TEST_SENTINEL' is not in the blocklist. With an empty blocklist and no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY in CI, scan-offensive returns rc=0 instead of rc=1, causing T2 and T5 to fail.
Changes: Have T2/T5 inject a temporary blocklist via env var (e.g. SCAN_OFFENSIVE_BLOCKLIST=/tmp/test-blocklist) with a synthetic pattern, or skip when no active patterns exist in the blocklist.


### FEATURE: fix: find-pr-by-marker.sh fail-closed short-form normalization breaks test F9 (2026-07-25, #1203)
Background: PR #1194 added `--repo` flag to `find-pr-by-marker.sh` with fail-closed behavior (exit 2) when short-form repo normalization via `gh repo view "$REPO_ARG"` fails. The test mock `tests/fixtures/gh-mock/gh` has no handler for `gh repo view <repo> --json owner,name`, so it returns exit 2 and test F9 (which exercises short-form resolution) now fails. The other three scripts changed in PR #1194 (issue-state-check.sh, wip-set-single.sh, ensure-board-card.sh) are unaffected — their passing tests do not exercise the short-form path. Three fix options: - (a) Add `owner,name` handler to the mock - (b) Change all four `--repo` scripts to use `gh repo view --json nameWithOwner` (consistent with existing mock handlers) - (c) Revert find-pr-by-marker.sh short-form to fail-open — leaves an integrity gap where a bad short-form slug silently falls back to the current repo Related to #1135
Changes: Implement whichever option is chosen — update `tests/fixtures/gh-mock/gh` and/or the affected script(s). All existing tests in `tests/feature-325-find-pr-by-marker.sh` must pass after the fix. Related to #1135


### FEATURE: fix(compute-staged-tests-token): pick invoking worktree, not first linked worktree with staged tests (concurrent-worktree stale-token blocks commits) (2026-07-25, #1240)
Background: `findRepoDirWithStagedTests()` in `bin/compute-staged-tests-token.js` returns the FIRST linked worktree with staged `tests/` files (it skips only the main worktree via `git worktree list --porcelain`), NOT the invoking/current worktree. On a machine with many concurrent worktrees (40+; 3 had staged tests simultaneously — 329-write-tests, migrate-repo-identity-guard, multirepo-safety), `/review-tests` records another worktree's fingerprint token. `workflow-gate`'s `hooks/workflow-gate/review-tests-checker.js` computes `computeStagedTestsToken(repoDir)` against the CURRENT commit worktree, so there is a permanent stale-token mismatch and every commit is blocked. Observed in session eddb6068 / PR #1239: the skill stored `2cdbc9c` (329-write-tests token) while the real token for the commit worktree was `c6e07106`; only a manual re-emit of the correct worktree-scoped token via `WORKFLOW_REVIEW_TESTS_COMPLETE` unblocked the commit. Distinct from #882 (the `process.cwd()`=main-worktree trigger) — this is the multi-worktree first-match failure mode of the same subsystem.
Changes: Scope `findRepoDirWithStagedTests()` to the invoking worktree instead of first-match across all worktrees: resolve `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` from CWD and prefer it (fall back to first-match only if the invoking dir has no staged tests). Add a regression test simulating 2+ worktrees with staged tests, asserting the token matches the invoking worktree's index. Related to #882. Related to #882
